## Purpose

`internal/core` is the orchestrator.
It allocates each cycle's number, dispatches the phases in order, and decides every transition and failure disposition.
It runs the deterministic floors that grade a phase's output before the next phase starts.
It also owns the batch-level blocker breaker and the boot-time recovery of a dirty main tree.
It moves build output a builder leaked into the main tree.
It carries audited work forward across fleet rebases and failed cycles, and connects the carryover memory to each cycle.

## Design

- **Each phase dispatch writes the input index of the cell first** (`input_index.go`, X5 of [ADR-0154](../adr/0154-the-pipeline-is-the-only-controller-and-each-agent-is-an-isolated-replaceable-worker.md), spec [llm-isolation.md](../llm-isolation.md) §8.5).
  - `cycleRun.dispatch` calls `writeInputIndex` before the loop of tries.
  - After a try with a verdict, `cycleRun.handoffRequests` gives the cell (`RequestCell`) to the port of `WithRequestHandoff`. `RequestHandoffWired` is the wiring proof of the composition root. So the next phase finds the result of a request in its index ([llm-isolation.md](../llm-isolation.md) §7.2.1). An error prints `WARN request channel of <phase>`, and the cycle goes on.
  - The item ids come from the `fleet_scope` of the cycle context. When the cycle already has a record in `.evolve/lineage/` with item ids, the record wins, also for a new run id. The new record keeps the item ids and the goal hash. It takes the new run id. The first dispatch writes the record before the index, so a failed index does not lose it.
  - At the start of a run, the host value of the fleet scope (`ipcenv.FleetScopeKey`) wins over `lane-scope.json`, and `materializeLaneScope` writes the file from it. `loadLaneScope`, `LaneScopeIDs` and `readLanePin` call `committedset`, so with a record, they return the record and never the file.
  - A record that cannot be written for a cycle with a fleet scope stops the dispatch with `ErrLaneRecordUnwritable`, so no cell starts. A failed index stays a WARN. `cycleRecord` takes the goal hash from the request, and it does not read the file.
  - When `dispatch` returns, on each path, `cycleRun.reportLanePinDrift` compares the file with the record (`committedset.LaneDrift`). When they differ, it emits `ISOLATION_LANE_PIN_FORGED` one time for each cycle. The signal gives both lists, the file path and the next step.
  - It reads `isolation.lineage_max` from the policy. Then it builds the index, writes the record and writes `<ws>/inputs/index.json`.
  - A failure removes the index of the earlier phase and prints `WARN input index for <phase>`, so the cell gets no index grants.
  - The phase request carries no index entry: the agent opens what it needs (`TestDispatch_TheIndexIsTheOnlyLineageInputAndNoEntryIsPreloadedIntoTheRequest`).
  - The handoff correction of `enforcedHandoffRejection` sends the agent to the `handoff-asks` entry of the index (`TestHandoffAsksTravelInTheIndex`).
  - `handoffRequestBlock` (`handoff_persona.go`) renders the request block of the pair into the task contract of tdd and build. It builds the request with `request.ForPair`. It uses the path lookup of `seedTaskContract` (`contractPathResolver`), so the block and the contract read the same item files (`TestSeedTaskContract_AFleetScopePathItemRendersTheSameAcceptanceInBothHalves`).
  - The block puts each ask text on one line, so inbox text cannot add a line (`TestHandoffRequestBlock_CollapsesNewlinesInAskText`).
  - `stampHandoffAuthor` runs first in `HandoffReviewer.Review`, and only for a phase that has a ledger pair. The tree digest is never computed for another phase (`TestHandoffReviewer_ATreeIsComputedOnlyForAPhaseWithALedgerPair`).
  - The stamp sets `author` from the dispatch record (`TestHandoffReviewer_ReplacesAnAgentWrittenAuthorWithTheDispatchRecord`). When no trustworthy record exists, it sets `author` to `{"cli":"unattributed"}` and logs one WARN (`TestHandoffReviewer_NoTrustworthyDispatchRecordReplacesTheAuthorWithUnattributedAndWarnsOnce`).
  - A call-log read error does not stop the digest stamp or the stale check (`TestHandoffReviewer_ACallLogReadErrorStillStampsTheDigest`).
  - The stamp sets `output_digest` from `worktreeContentSHA` when the ledger has none. It writes the round of that stamp in the key `stamp`, which only the pipeline writes.
  - A different digest makes the check outcome `invalid` when `round` did not rise above the stamped round (`TestHandoffReviewer_TheSameRoundOnANewTreeIsInvalid`, `TestHandoffReviewer_AStaleDigestMakesTheCheckInvalidAndNamesIt`). A higher `round` gets a fresh stamp (`TestHandoffReviewer_AnInPlaceEditThatRaisesTheRoundIsStampedFresh`).
- **A fleet lane finds the quota-paused cycle of its todo** (`lane_resume.go`). `FindLaneResume` returns the checkpoint only when the newest cycle of the todo scope paused on a quota wall and the checkpoint is still resumable. `EmitLaneResumed` publishes `ORCHESTRATOR_LANE_RESUMED`. The flow and the conditions are in [checkpoint-resume.md](../checkpoint-resume.md#fleet-lane-resume).
- **A resume pends a carrier that a crash left at HEAD** (`resume_carrier_heal.go`, ADR-0105 B1). The unwind and the debugger re-entry each commit a carrier with the trailer `Evolve-Carrier: cycle-<N>/<run>`, rebase it, then pend it. After a crash between the rebase and the pend, a resumed ship re-audits an empty `git diff HEAD`.
  - `loadResumeBootstrap` calls `healCarrierAtHead` before a phase runs. It pends the change on its fork point only when the trailer names this cycle and run. It never acts in an in-place worktree, outside a git checkout or during a rebase. In a git checkout, a git failure fails the resume.
  - The heal does not move `WorktreeBaseSHA`. The resumed cycle sees a pending change on the new base. The tree-binding check of ship then decides the next phase, as for the W0 split of the design review.
  - Pinned by `TestLoadResumeBootstrap_PendsARebasedCarrierBeforeAnyPhaseRuns` and `TestCarrierAtHead_RecognisesOnlyThisCycleAndRunsCarrier`.
- **The routing order is the one successor authority, live and on resume.** The code is in `resume_transition.go`. The live walk takes the successor of an inserted phase from `cfg.Order` (`nextInOrder`). A resume of an inserted phase does the same: `nextInOrderRun` walks `cfg.Order` from the phase and skips each optional phase that `routing-plan.json` marks `run:false`.
  - `routing-plan.json` is the raw plan of the router, before the floor clamp turns mandatory phases back on. Thus `resumeFloor` keeps `build`, `audit`, `ship`, each phase of the ship floor and each phase in `cfg.Mandatory`. A run:false entry never skips a kept phase, on the order walk and on the plan-array fallback. `tdd` is kept unless its conditional rule unpins it, as `router.ClampPlanToFloorWith` does. When the signals cannot be read, `tdd` stays kept.
  - The archetype degrade goes to `build` until build is complete.
  - A last guard, `holdShipFloor`, checks the result of each path. When the result is `ship` and `build` or `audit` is not in `CompletedPhases`, the resume goes to the missing phase. Thus `ship ⇒ build ∧ audit` holds on every resume path, whatever the order or the plan holds.
  - Pinned by `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanCannotSkipTheIntegrityFloor`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanCannotSkipAuditOrShip`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanCannotSkipAPinnedTDD`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ATrivialCycleUnpinsTDDAsTheFloorClampDoes`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanCannotSkipAConfiguredMandatoryPhase`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanFallbackCannotSkipAConfiguredMandatoryPhase` `TestDegradeInsertedSuccessor_GoesToBuildUntilBuildIsComplete`, `TestHoldShipFloor_ShipWaitsForBuildAndAudit`, `TestResolveResumeNext_TheDegradeNeverReachesShipBeforeAudit` and `TestResolveResumeNext_APhaseOutsideTheOrderFallsBackToThePlan`.
  - The resume reproduces only the skips of optional phases that the plan records (`run:false`). The live walk can also skip a phase by a router decision, and a resume does not repeat that decision. The walk reads `cfg.Order` once, so a name that occurs twice cannot loop.
  - `nextFromRoutingPlan` reads the plan's array order. It is a fallback only for a phase that is not in the order. An example is a minted phase that a resumed process did not register again. The archetype degrade (`degradeInsertedSuccessor`) is the last fallback.
  - Before this change, a resume took the plan's array order first, and a no-plan resume degraded by archetype. `bug-reproduction` is an Evaluate phase that runs before build, so a no-plan resume sent it to audit and skipped build. That is the red-test-never-greened shape of the [cycle-1550 incident](../../incidents/2026-08-24-anchor-order-splice-misslot.md).
  - Pinned by `TestResolveResumeNext_ANoPlanResumeOfAPreBuildEvaluatePhaseGoesToBuild`, `TestResolveResumeNext_TheRoutingOrderOutranksThePlanArrayOrder`, `TestResolveResumeNext_TheOrderWalkSkipsWhatThePlanDoesNotRun`, `TestResolveResumeNext_APhaseOutsideTheOrderFallsBackToThePlan`, `TestResolveResumeNext_TheLastPhaseOfTheOrderEndsTheCycle` and `TestResolveResumeNext_APlanThatRunsNoLaterOptionalPhaseEndsTheCycle`.
- **A mint batch is routed once** (`routing_dispatch.go`). `registerMintedPhases` registers each minted spec, then calls `phasespec.ApplyUserRouting` once for the batch. Thus a minted phase anchored to a later batch-mate gets the fixpoint placement, as discovered user phases do. Each mint warning is a `CONFIG_PHASE_ROUTING_DEFECT` signal: a rejected mint, a mint that clashes with a runner, a merge warning or a routing warning. Pinned by `TestRegisterMintedPhases_ABatchMateAnchorResolvesThroughTheFixpoint`, `TestRegisterMintedPhases_AnUnresolvableAnchorIsACodedSignal` and `TestRegisterMintedPhases_ARejectedOrClashingMintIsACodedSignal`.
- **Cycle-number allocation.** `StateUpdater` is an optional storage capability, kept apart from `Storage` so every existing fake and adapter stays valid.
  - With it, `allocateCycle` mints `max(LastCycleNumber, LastAllocatedCycleNumber, highest occupied go/acs/cycle<N>) + 1` through `UpdateState`.
  - It runs before any artifact exists under that number.
  - Without it, the orchestrator uses `LastCycleNumber + 1` above the same source floor.
  - Resume never allocates: `RunCycleFromPhase` carries the cycle from the run record.
- **Cycle-end persist is a merge, not a write.** On a `StateUpdater`, `persistCycleEndState` replaces the state with the run's outcome.
  - It max-merges the lease and `LastCycleNumber` with disk and keeps the disk revision (`UpdateState` increments it).
  - It unions `FailedAt` and `CarryoverTodos` with what peers wrote, because `EVOLVE_FLEET` skips the global lock.
- **Audit-FAIL disposition at the audit chokepoint** (`decideAfterAuditFail`, [ADR-0093](../adr/0093-retry-envelope-and-terminal-retro.md)).
  - It builds and writes the failure dossier, reads the audit's declared class, and computes the retry envelope from the policy table.
  - It consults the `RetryAdjudicator` Strategy only when more than one action is legal.
  - The proposal is clamped to the envelope. A granted retry re-enters `tdd` or `build` through `reentryPhase`.
  - A decline goes to retro, which is the terminal learning step once per cycle.
  - A floor halt still routes to retro so the cycle gets its post-mortem, and `SystemFailureSignal` stops the loop.
  - `emitAuditRepairDecision` makes one coded signal: WARN `CodeAuditRepairDeclined`, or INFO `CodeAuditRepairGranted` with the try number.
- **The audit_fix round runs after the audit-FAIL disposition** (`audit_fix_round.go`, [ADR-0158](../adr/0158-a-repair-round-runs-only-for-a-finding-the-lane-can-fix-and-the-auditor-applies-a-small-prescribed-fix.md) decisions 7 to 13). `recordAndBranch` gives the branch of `decideAfterAuditFail` to `cycleRun.auditFixRound`. The round records the `LADDER_AUDIT_FIX` choice and asks the registered `audit_fix` `RungHandler`. The handler result changes the branch:
  - `Handled` without `Escalate` (`passed` or `held_proposal`): the next phase is `audit`, through the decision-only edge `audit→audit`. The audit runs again on the patched tree, so ship binds a fresh audit verdict. No tdd or build round runs. The reason prefix `audit-fix: ` spends no repair try.
  - `Escalate`: the repair round of today runs. At `workflow.retry_ladder.stage` `enforce`, its first dispatch is the raised build with the L6 digest.
  - `Stop`: the next phase is retro. The reason has the hand-off prefix and `audit_fix_exhausted`, so `sealHandedOff` seals the cycle `handed-off` with the salvage pointer.
  - Any other result, a handler error (a WARN line), no registered handler, a system halt and a fixability stop keep the branch of today.
- The round chooses the rung only when the branch is a repair round. It asks the handler again at each later audit FAIL of the cycle, also before retro. Only the record of the handler knows that an escalation failed. The choice moved from the first tdd or build dispatch (`ladderForDispatch`) to this round, so the dispatch no longer records `LADDER_AUDIT_FIX`. `Orchestrator.RegisteredRungs` lists the rungs that have a handler, and `TestWireOrchestratorDeps_RegistersTheAuditFixHandler` proves that the cycle registers `audit_fix`. Pinned by `TestAuditFixRound_APassedPatchRunsTheAuditAgainAndNoRepairRound`, `TestAuditFixRound_AnEscalationRunsTheRepairRoundOfToday`, `TestAuditFixRound_AFailedEscalationSealsTheCycleHandedOff`, `TestAuditFixRound_ShadowRecordsAndKeepsTheFlow`, `TestAuditFixRound_KeepsTheFlowWhenNothingCanRun` and `TestRunCycle_APassedAuditFixShipsAfterASecondAuditWithNoRepairRound`.
- **A build floor residual can start the audit_fix round** (`build_floor_residual.go`, [ADR-0158 addendum of 2026-11-02](../adr/0158-a-repair-round-runs-only-for-a-finding-the-lane-can-fix-and-the-auditor-applies-a-small-prescribed-fix.md#addendum-2026-11-02-the-build-floor-residual-source)). `reviewDeliverable` gives each review a new `floorResidualLog` through `ReviewInput`. Two floor checks add a typed `FloorResidual` to it: `coverChangedFunctionFailures` for each refused function, and `explanationDocumentationFailures` for each Changed Areas omission. `ReviewResult.Residuals` carries them.
  - `rejectAfterCorrections` calls `floorResidualAuditFix` before it aborts a build. The round runs only when each floor failure has a residual.
  - `floorResidualBlock` makes one defect and one prescription for each residual. `runAuditFix` then runs the round of `auditFixRound` with the source `build_floor`.
  - A pass delivers the build, a stop seals the cycle `handed-off`, and each other result keeps the abort. Pinned by the `TestBuildFloorResidual_` tests and `TestReviewDeliverable_CarriesTheTypedResidualsOfItsOwnReview`.
- **A decline to a defer-or-quarantine class ends the cycle after retro** (`reintent_decision.go`). `auditDeclineDefers` holds when `CycleState.AuditDeclineReason` is set and the decision is `defer-or-quarantine`. The audit's declared class gives the decision through the policy table. When the class does not, the retro's `failure-decision.json` gives it. `decideAfterRetro` then returns `PhaseEnd` with the `audit-fail-defer: ` reason, after the floor gates and before the bookkeeping regrade.
- **The closeout gets the defer that ended the cycle as a value** (`reintent_decision.go`). When the retro decision holds the `audit-fail-defer: ` reason, `carryDefer` copies the category and the `fix_type` of `auditDeclineDefers` into `CycleResult.DeferCategory` and `DeferFixType`. The live path and the resume path both do this. `DeferOf(result)` gives the values to `cycleoutcome`, so the closeout reads no workspace file (`TestRecordAndBranch_TheDeferThatEndsTheCycleIsCarriedOnTheResult`, `TestResumeExecution_TheDeferThatEndsAResumedCycleIsCarriedOnTheResult`). When the reason is a defer but no defer resolves, `carryDefer` writes a WARN.
  - `deferDecision` has three sources in this order. The first is the declared class of the audit through the policy table. The second is the build's system class, which `failureDossier` computes on each call. It reads the auditor ledger (host evidence) and `build-report.md` (agent-written), never `failure-dossier.json`. The third is a trusted `failure-decision.json`.
  - All three sources are files that agents write. The host makes sure that each file is fresh for this cycle, but it does not prove the content. Before each retro dispatch on the live path and the resume path, `clearRetroDecisionInput` removes any `failure-decision.json`. Thus only this retro writes the file that the orchestrator reads (`TestDispatch_TheRetroNeverSeesADecisionPlantedBeforeIt`, `TestResumeExecution_AResumedRetroNeverSeesADecisionPlantedBeforeIt`). When the removal fails, `CycleState.FailureDecisionUntrusted` is set, and the defer rule and the retro floor ignore the file (`TestClearRetroDecisionInput_ADecisionTheHostCannotRemoveIsNeverRead`).
  - A planted read-only `failure-dossier.json` that claims a system class does not defer the item (`TestAuditDeclineDefers_APlantedReadOnlyDossierClaimingASystemClassDoesNotDefer`). `writeDossier` writes through `atomicwrite.Bytes`, which renames a temporary file over the entry. Thus a planted symlink is replaced and its target stays the same (`TestWriteDossier_APlantedSymlinkIsReplacedAndItsTargetIsUntouched`). A failed dossier write is a WARN.
- **The router cannot overrule a defer** (`decideAfterRetroRouted`). It returns before the router and writes one `routing-decision-<n>.json` with the category, the fix type and the source. One `routing_decision` ledger row binds it. So no router proposal can start a tdd, build or audit round on the same diff. Cycle 1953 is why: the router proposed `retry` after an `intent-malformed` decline, and the cycle ran tdd, a 23-minute build and an identical audit FAIL. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_Cycle1953ReintentDecisionEndsTheCycle`, `TestRecordAndBranch_Cycle1953ReintentDecisionBreaksTheLoop`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_ADeferDecisionOutranksTheBookkeepingRegrade`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_TheRetroFailureDecisionAloneDefersAfterADecline`, `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_AFloorHaltOutranksADeferDecision` and `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_ADeclineWithoutADeferDecisionKeepsTheRetroRoute`.
- **A FAIL that names only the explanation document re-authors it at Build** (`explanation_correction.go`, [logic-first design §5.14](../logic-first-delivery-design.md)).
  - `auditFailEnvelope` reads the audit's failure block once.
  - `explanationCorrectionDocument` locates each defect by its first path-like token.
  - A token needs a slash or a stem of two or more characters with an extension starting with a letter.
  - Thus `e.g.` and `2.5` are prose, while `probe.c` is a file. A `:line` or `#L` suffix and quotes or brackets are trimmed.
  - When every location is `explanationdocs.DocumentPath` and `CycleState.AuditFailReasons` is empty, the FAIL is `explanation-needs-correction`.
  - `explanationCorrectionEnvelope` narrows a granted envelope to `retry@explanation` or `decline` (`computeRetryEnvelope` stays unchanged; a decline or halt passes through).
  - `retry@explanation` re-enters Build through `reentryPhase`, skips TDD, keeps the `code-audit-fail` budget, and clamps `retry@tdd`.
  - Choosing it writes nothing: the round's brief reads the defects from `auditRejectionReasons`.
  - The first landing copied them into `audit-fail-reason.json` (`recordExplanationCorrection`).
  - Removing that copy ensures a re-author Build that dies before re-audit is digested from its own failure.
  - `seedAuditRepairContext` adds `CtxKeyExplanationReauthor` to that round's Build dispatch on both roots, scoping the prompt to the document.
  - Any defect located elsewhere keeps today's routing.
  - Pinned by `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_AnExplanationOnlyFailReauthorsAtBuildNotTDD`, `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_AMixedFailKeepsTDD` and `TestRunCycle_AnExplanationOnlyAuditFailReDispatchesOnlyTheBuildReauthor`.
- **A passing audit's `NEEDS_CORRECTION` is a document-only rung, not a FAIL** (`explanation_correction_rung.go`, X2, [logic-first design §5.15](../logic-first-delivery-design.md)).
  - Both dispatch roots call `decideExplanationCorrection` after the audit FAIL decision. The trigger is a PASS or WARN audit whose `## Explanation Documentation` reads `NEEDS_CORRECTION` (`pendingExplanationCorrection`).
  - The rung appends the Evidence to `CycleState.ExplanationCorrections`, sets `ExplanationCorrectionActive` and schedules Build.
  - `seedAuditRepairContext` gives that Build `CtxKeyExplanationReauthor` and `CtxKeyExplanationCorrection`. The brief lists the document lines the Evidence cites, then the Evidence (`explanationCorrectionBrief`).
  - The Build that completes clears the flag, and the audit runs again on the corrected document.
  - The budget is the `MaxRetries` of `policy.CategoryExplanationCorrection` (default 1), never `code-audit-fail`. Each round emits INFO `ORCHESTRATOR_EXPLANATION_CORRECTION_RUNG`. A spent budget ships the verified code with WARN `ORCHESTRATOR_EXPLANATION_CORRECTION_EXHAUSTED`.
  - A FAIL verdict never takes the rung. `dossierParams` copies the rounds into the dossier.
  - Pinned by `TestRunCycle_ANeedsCorrectionOnAVerifiedBuildCorrectsTheDocumentOnceAndShipsWithNoAuditFail`, `TestRunCycle_ANeedsCorrectionPastItsBudgetShipsTheVerifiedCodeAndSignalsTheExhaustion`, `TestRunCycle_TheCorrectionBudgetComesFromTheFailurePolicy`, `TestRunCycle_AFailingAuditWithANeedsCorrectionReviewTakesTheFailRouteNotTheRung`, `TestResumePath_ANeedsCorrectionOnAPassingAuditTakesTheSameRung` and `TestRunCycle_TheDossierRecordsEachCorrection`.
- **A post-Build writer that leaves the explanation stale routes back to Build; it does not abort** (`build_explanation_handoff.go`).
  - After a later source writer (tdd in an audit-repair round, test-amplification), both dispatch roots check `explanationRefreshEligible` and call `explanationdocs.RefreshResult`.
  - Such a writer can change the whole-diff digest without changing the material behavior the Builder documented.
  - The host re-seals its snapshot once the writer's deliverable is final.
  - Each root passes the outcome to `routeAfterExplanationRefresh`, which schedules what it returns or aborts with what it returns.
  - A material-scope change (`requiresBuild`) re-enters Build, unbudgeted, so the Builder rewrites the rationale.
  - A failure classed as content (`errors.Is(err, explanationdocs.ErrContent)`) re-enters Build for re-authoring: missing, malformed or oversized docs or Changed Areas drift.
  - When every legal successor of the writer leads to Build (`StateMachine.everyPathReaches`), the round is free.
  - TDD's only successors are build-planner and build, so Build runs anyway and re-validates the document.
  - Any other writer spends one `AuditRepairAttempts` from `code-audit-fail`, read from `computeRetryEnvelope` (legal only while it offers `retry@build`).
  - That bound keeps a post-Build writer from looping.
  - A spent budget aborts as `ErrCycleLevelFailure` (`wrapCycleLevelError`), ending the cycle so the batch continues.
  - The message names the envelope's reason (`… no explanation re-author round left: retry budget spent for code-audit-fail (2/2)`).
  - A binding or unreadable failure (activation, host snapshot, git, Build report, non-regular doc, manifest write) is not `ErrContent` and aborts raw.
  - Every taken round emits one WARN `phase.outcome` `ORCHESTRATOR_EXPLANATION_REAUTHOR_ROUTED` with `fields.next` set to Build and `fields.charged`.
  - A charged round adds `fields.attempt` and `fields.envelope`.
  - `Event.Attempt` stays unset because a routing disposition is not a dispatch try, and `Verdict` stays empty so the dashboard skips it.
  - `routeAfterExplanationRefresh` may spend, signal and print, so it is a command rather than a query.
  - The rung never sets `AuditRepairActive`.
  - In cycle 1822's shape an audit had already spoken, so the flag was true and Build carries that audit's brief.
  - A round before any audit leaves it false, and Build carries no repair brief.
  - `archiveRepairPrompts` numbers archives by `AuditRepairAttempts`.
  - A free tdd round keeps the repair round's number (`build-prompt.round1.txt`); a charged round archives as `round<N+1>`.
  - `explanationdocs.checkBuild` classifies content verdicts and faults on separate channels; this package only disposes of them.
  - Pinned by `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_AStaleCitationAfterTDDRoutesToBuildWithoutSpendingTheBudget`, `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_AStaleCitationAfterABudgetedWriterSpendsOneRepairAttempt`, `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_ABudgetedWriterPastItsBudgetEndsTheCycleNotTheBatch` and `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_BindingMismatchStillAbortsAsBatchFatal`.
  - Pinned also by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AStaleCitationAfterAResumedTDDRoutesToBuildWithoutSpending`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AResumedRefreshAbortStopsBeforeBuild`, `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_AMaterialScopeChangeAfterAWriterRoutesToBuildWithoutCharge`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AResumedMaterialScopeChangeRoutesToBuild` and `TestStateMachine_EveryPathReaches`.
- **A FAIL that only deterministic gates forced repairs at Build, led by the gate's remediation** (`audit_gate_remedy.go`, [incident](../../incidents/2026-10-07-cycle-1828-gate-forced-fail-earned-no-repair-and-halted.md)).
  - `auditGateRemedies` is the one table of the audit's deterministic gates.
  - Each row holds the diagnostic's stable prefix, the one-step remediation and `isStaticReading`.
  - For skills-drift, the remediation runs `EVOLVE_WORKTREE_ROOT=<worktree> go run ./cmd/evolve skills generate` in `<worktree>/go`.
  - Every derived block declares `gateDerivedClass` (`code-audit-fail`).
  - `gateFailureBlock` derives a `phasecontract.FailureBlock` from `CycleState.AuditFailReasons` only when deterministic gates alone forced FAIL over a PASS or WARN narrative.
  - Each defect reads `<remediation> — <diagnosis>`; any other reason or missing conflict record gives no block.
  - A reason carrying `HarnessRedClauseMarker` (an EGPS red the harness cannot run) is no gate's diagnosis.
  - `auditFailEnvelope` uses the derived block only when the audit report declares no block of its own.
  - It narrows a granted envelope to `retry@build` or `decline` (`gateRemediationEnvelope` through `narrowRetryEnvelope`, shared with `explanationCorrectionEnvelope`).
  - `auditRejectionReasons` renders the derived block first under the same condition so the repair brief and adjudicator lead with remediation.
  - A declared block keeps the brief it had.
  - The table lives in `core` because the retro floor reads it too, and `phases/audit` renders remedies from `AuditGateRemedy` and `HarnessRedClauseMarker`.
  - `AuditGateOf` is exported so `phases/audit/audit_gate_remedy_binding_test.go` can feed every real producer's diagnostic through it.
  - Pinned by `TestAuditGateFail_SkillsDriftOverPassNarrativeGrantsOneBuildRepairLedByItsRemediation`, `TestAuditGateFail_TheDerivedRouteIsRetryAtBuild`, `TestAuditGateFail_AFailNarrativeBesideAGateDiagnosisDerivesNothingAndKeepsTheFloor` and `TestAuditGateFail_AHarnessRedEGPSEarnsNoRepairAndKeepsTheHalt`.
  - Pinned also by `TestResumePath_GateForcedAuditFailRepairsAtBuildWithinTheBudget`, `TestAuditFailEnvelope_AnAuditorDeclaredClassKeepsItsFullEnvelope`, `TestAuditRejectionReasons_AnAuditorDeclaredBlockKeepsTheDerivedGateBlockOut` and `TestGateFailureBlock_DerivesTheClassOnlyWhenGatesAloneForcedTheFail`.
- **Round-scoped audit artifacts** (`audit_round_artifacts.go`).
  - The auditor persona pre-writes `acs-verdict.json`, and `audit.Classify` honors a pre-staged file.
  - Every audit re-dispatch (repair round, bookkeeping regrade, ship-error recovery, debugger `RERUN_PHASE`) renames `acs-verdict.json` and `audit-report.md` to `*.round<N>.*`.
  - `supersedePreviousAuditRound` sits in the audit pre-dispatch block of both dispatch surfaces beside `resetFloorFailReason`, never at a branch site.
  - The round index is the greater of `AuditDispatches` and the count of completed audit phases.
  - The full design is in [the incident record](../../incidents/2026-09-02-repair-round-stale-acs-verdict.md).
- **Bookkeeping regrade** (`bookkeeping_regrade.go`, [ADR-0086](../adr/0086-bookkeeping-regrade-microcycle.md)). The classifier owns the reason prefixes (`defect ledger: `, `closure claim without a citation: `, and the anchored `verdict-conflict: auditor narrative=PASS|WARN` record). The producers in `internal/phases/audit` are pinned to these matchers by `phases/audit/bookkeeping_reason_singlesource_test.go`, which feeds real minted diagnostics through them. The target phase resolves through the `bookkeeping-regrade` RecoveryMap key, so catalog config may remap it; the compiled fallback is `audit`.
- **Blocker breaker** (`blocker_breaker.go`).
  - It is a pure, deterministic function over a batch's failure digests, run by the loop after each iteration.
  - Rules in order: `guard-class` (guard aborts fail by construction), `unexplained-failures` (digests without a reason), `identical-fingerprint` (Rule B), and `consecutive-failures`.
  - The first rule that trips names the halt.
  - The breaker is task-agnostic: same-task repeats belong to S5 quarantine (`task_retry_ceiling`), so distinct rejections never trip it.
  - A zero ceiling disables a rule inside `BlockerBreakerConfig`; policy cannot pass zero because a non-positive value keeps the default.
  - After digest rules, `EvaluateLaneDeferrals` runs `lane-deferrals` over the batch's lane-deferred cycles.
- **The breaker counts a failure class as one identity.** The `identical-fingerprint` rule counts a digest by its class fingerprint when it has one (`breakerIdentity`). Else it counts the exact fingerprint. An ack of either fingerprint removes the digest from the count ([failure-identity-lifecycle.md §4](../failure-identity-lifecycle.md#4-failure-class-fingerprint)).
- **A claimed item keeps a readable contract path** (`task_contract.go`). `taskItemRefs` takes each id's path from `fleet_scope_paths`, which is written before the claim. The claim then moves the record into `processing/cycle-N/`, so that path no longer opens. When a pair path is not readable, `taskItemRefs` asks the scope-path resolver again, and the resolver returns the `processing/` path of a claimed item (`inboxmover.ResolveDispatchState`). Before this, cycles 1946 and 1950 told the builder `inbox record unreadable` in every correction prompt, so it never saw the acceptance criteria. Pinned by `TestTaskItemRefs_StalePendingPathReResolvesToTheClaimedRecord`, `TestResolveDispatchState_ProcessingCarriesTheClaimedPath` and `TestScopePathResolver_ClaimedItemResolvesToItsProcessingPath`.
- **A reason's detail never moves its identity.**
  - `AssembleFailureDigest` classifies and fingerprints fail reasons with their `cyclestate` detail stripped (`withoutDetails`).
  - `normalizeReasonForFingerprint` strips it for every caller, including contract-block identity, and `carryover.Summary` strips it from failure-learning records.
  - Thus a refusal that names its paths keeps one class and one fingerprint across path sets.
  - Pinned by `TestAssembleFailureDigest_AReasonsDetailNeverChangesItsIdentity` and `TestNormalizeReasonForFingerprint_ContractBlocksShareAnIdentityAcrossTheirDetail`.
- **Fingerprint ack ledger.**
  - `.evolve/resolved-fingerprints.json` is an append-only JSON array (`fingerprint`, `resolved_at`, `resolved_by`).
  - `LoadResolvedFingerprints` feeds `BlockerBreakerConfig.AckedFingerprints`.
  - It is written by `evolve loop --reset --fingerprint` (`AppendResolvedFingerprint`) and `ConsumePipelineDefectFingerprint`.
  - `ConsumePipelineDefectFingerprint` parses the fingerprint from an item's `consumed_by` text and falls back to its auto-filed `notes`.
- **Boot recovery** (`boot_preflight.go`).
  - A leak into the main tree makes later cycle tree-diff guards FAIL until someone stashes by hand.
  - `runLoop`'s boot path calls `QuarantineDirtyTree`, which moves leaked tracked source into a named stash (`git stash pop` recovers it).
  - It also calls `ShipSHAMismatch`, which reports `SELF_SHA_TAMPERED` drift at boot instead of only at ship.
  - The wiring lives in `cmd/evolve/cmd_loop_boot_recovery.go`.
- **A red composed gate runs on main before a re-audit** (`composed_gate_attribution.go`).
  - `declineComposedGates` reads the named failed tests of each red gate. `BaseRedAttributor` runs them on main with the gate's flags.
  - When main also fails each test, `parkOnMainRed` parks the lane and `cycleRun.parkedOr` returns the main-red park error.
  - An unnamed test, a lane-only red or a base-run error keeps the re-audit.
  - Pinned by `TestComposedGate_ARedThatMainAlsoFailsParksTheLane`, `TestComposedGate_ARedOnlyTheLaneHasGoesToReaudit`, `TestComposedGate_ABaseRunErrorKeepsTheRouteOfToday`, `TestComposedGate_ARedWithNoNamedTestIsNotRunOnMain` and `TestComposedGate_OneRedOnEachSideParksOnlyWhenEveryGateIsRedOnMain`.
  - Pinned also by `TestComposedGate_ARedThatMainAlsoFailsEndsTheCycleAsParkedOnEachRebaseRoute`, `TestComposedGate_AResumedShipThatParksReturnsTheParkError`, `TestComposedPark_TheCycleEndsWithTheMainRedParkError`, `TestComposedPark_AnUnparkedCycleKeepsItsError` and `TestComposedPark_ADefaultBaseRunnerRunsGoTest`.
- **Build handoff floor** (`build_floor_reviewer.go`).
  - Deterministic checks run during Build verification so a red floor triggers a bounded in-phase correction before audit and review.
  - The reviewer is `BuildHandoffFloor.Review`, which owns policy only; each check is a `BuildFloorCheckFn` (`DefaultBuildFloorChecks` in production).
  - The engine composes:
  - `RemovalClaimFailures`, `PlaceholderTokenFailures` and `personaBudgetFailures`, which run outside `changedPackageFloorChecks` because zero-package diffs return early.
  - Both deletion-only builds and persona-only lanes produce zero packages.
  - `testAuditFailures` (`build_testaudit_check.go`), the test-audit ratchet on the changed packages. Main CI ran `TestTreeRatchet` alone, so three lanes shipped an `error-text-match` finding with no baseline row and turned main red ([incident](../../incidents/2026-11-20-a-lane-shipped-error-text-tests-and-turned-main-red.md)).
    - **The paths** come from `worktreePathsSince` with `--no-renames`. A moved package keeps its old path, so the floor reports its stale rows. A git error gives one floor line. The check never passes on a path list that it cannot read.
    - **The scope** is `testaudit.AuditScope`. A changed package goes to `testaudit.CheckPackages`, which gives the same rows as `CheckTree` for that package. The config comes from `testaudit.LoadConfig` on the worktree, so a lane is judged by its own config. A worktree with no audit config is skipped.
    - **A change to the audit rules, the config or the central baselines** sets `Whole`. The floor then runs the `./internal/testaudit` package in the worktree through `buildSelfCheckRunner`. That run checks the whole tree. Thus a red row on main in an untouched package also blames the lane. This case is rare and console-routed.
    - **The lines** tell the lane what to do. A new finding: remove the weak pattern, because Rebaseline refuses new rows. A stale row: run `evolve test audit --rebaseline <package>`. The kind is read with `errors.Is` and `errors.As`, never from the message text. Pinned by the `TestStaticBuildFloorChecks_*TestAudit*` tests and `TestTestAuditFailures_*`.
  - `docsFloorWarn`, the [ADR-0077](../adr/0077-docs-floor-for-architecture-changes.md) floor. It labels with the blocking-grade classifier `docsfloor.IsArchitectureClass`, which drops test-only diffs (the WARN's main false positive), but its verdict stays WARN.
  - `commentFloorFailures` (`comment_floor.go`), the comment regrowth guard.
    - It counts added comment lines across the diff with `commentaudit.AddedAcrossDiff` (`--no-renames`), so moved or renamed comments are spared.
    - Nothing under `docs/` is code, so archived ancestor predicates under `docs/private/research/` add no comment (`TestCommentFloorFailures_AnAdoptedAncestorsArchivedPredicatesAddNoComment`).
    - Its stage is `policy.json` `comment_floor.stage` (`CommentFloorConfig`, compiled default `shadow`), read with `config.GateStage`.
    - Shadow WARNs, enforce rejects with every added line named, off is silent, and an unknown word is off with a WARN.
    - An unreadable diff fails open with a WARN, and git errors stay in the error without printing.
    - `changedWorktreePathsSince` lists paths with `-z` so non-ASCII names keep git's raw path.
    - Pinned by `TestCommentFloorFailures_ACommittedBuildIsJudgedAgainstItsBase`, `TestCommentFloorFailures_WithoutAPolicyBlockItOnlyShadows`, `TestCommentFloorFailures_ANewFilesPackageDocIsNotAdded` and `TestCommentFloorFailures_AnUnreadableDiffFailsOpen`.
    - Pinned also by `TestCommentFloorStage_AnUnknownWordIsOff`, `TestWorktreeGit_CarriesGitsOwnErrorInsteadOfPrintingIt`, `TestChangedWorktreePathsSince_NamesANonASCIIPathAsGitStoresIt`, `TestCommentFloorResult_EnforceRejectsEveryAddedCommentByName`, `TestCommentFloorResult_ShadowOnlyWarnsAndOffOrACleanDiffIsSilent`, `TestCommentFloorFailures_JudgesTheWorktreeAgainstItsBaseAndSparesAMovedComment` and `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_RunsTheCommentFloor`.
  - `sizeFloorFailures` (`size_floor.go`), the size-ratchet floor against the base: `sizeratchet.CheckAgainstBase` reads the offender list and the changed `.go` files at the base (`commentaudit.ReadAtBase`). It measures the same files on disk. It refuses a listed function that grew past its base size, and a new function past 50 lines. So a lane cannot regrow a function before the boundary `evolve ratchet tighten` removes the slack. A module without a list at the base is silent, and an unreadable base, diff or module prefix fails open with a WARN. Pinned by `TestSizeFloorFailures_RefusesAListedFunctionThatRegrowsIntoItsSlack`, `TestSizeFloorFailures_IsSilentWithoutAWorktreeOrAListAndFailsOpenOnAnUnreadableBase`, `TestSizeFloorFailures_JudgesAnUncleanWorktreePathAgainstItsModule` and the wiring proof `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_RunsTheSizeFloor`.
    - **The refusal** tells the lane to shrink the function back to its base size and not to edit the list. It does not name the writer, so it sends no lane to run it.
    - **The module prefix** is `filepath.Rel(worktree, codequality.ModuleDir(worktree))` (`moduleRelPrefix`), so an unclean worktree path still maps `go/p/a.go` to the module.
    - **No stage dial, by design.** A clean tree passes by construction: every listed function is at or under its allowance at the base. The refusal is exact and cheap to satisfy. A shadow period lets a lane consume slack before the tighten, which is what this floor exists to stop (R4 of the logic-first design). The comment floor has a dial because it started with an existing debt; this floor starts with none.
  - `LintFloorChecks` (`lint_floor.go`), the golangci-lint floor. It exists because a lane shipped an unchecked `f.Close` in wave 171. The local floor ran no lint, CI failed, and main went red. The check takes a `LintFn` and the composition root injects `commitgate.LintPackages`, the same pattern as `RepoContractFloorChecks`. Thus the manual commit gate and the floor share one runner. Only the build floor runs the judge, so `lintFloorJudge` is not exported and has no `gateregistry` entry.
    - **The package set** is the changed Go packages (`changedGoTestPackages`) after `buildTagVisiblePackages`. The lint uses the `go/.golangci.yml` that CI uses. It does not use `ciparity.TierScope`, because CI lints every package.
    - **A finding** is a floor RED. The line names the packages, each `file:line` of the finding (the text is cut at 1500 characters) and the `golangci-lint run` command that reproduces it.
    - **A tool that is missing or fails to run** fails open. The floor writes a WARN, emits the signal `BUILD_LINT_FLOOR_SKIPPED` with `fields.cause`, and passes. The CI lint job stays the backstop. The signal makes a fleet-wide skip visible, for example a version skew between the local tool and `go/.golangci.yml`.
    - **A lock-wait timeout** is a RED with `class: retryable`, not a pass. The lint did not judge the code, so the line tells the lane to build again.
    - **The derived floor** (`derived_floor.go`) is `DerivedFloorChecks`. It takes a `DerivedRunFn`, which binds a generator to the worktree, and the composition root injects `core.DerivedWorktree(worktree).Run`. It calls `StaleDerivedFindings` on the paths of the floor diff, and the ship gate calls the same function. The rules are in [internal-derived.md](internal-derived.md). A check that cannot run fails open with the signal `BUILD_DERIVED_FLOOR_SKIPPED`. Pinned by the `TestDerivedFloor_` tests and `TestProductionBuildFloorChecks_CorrectsAStaleDerivedFileByItsGenerateVerb`.
    - **The cost** was measured on 2026-11-14: a warm run on `./internal/core ./internal/commitgate ./internal/derived ./cmd/evolve` took 8.4 seconds of wall time. A run on one small package took under 1 second. Pinned by the `TestLintFloor_` tests and `TestProductionBuildFloorChecks_CorrectsALintFindingByFileAndLine`.
  - `changedPackageFloorChecks`, which reuses the post-build selfcheck machinery (`floorTestPackages` → `runBuildSelfCheck`). Packages enrolled in `.apicover-enforce` run once, under the coverage-instrumented pass in `apicoverNamingFailures`. That pass doubles as their selfcheck and applies CI's apicover naming floor at handoff. The rest take the plain selfcheck. The floor writes `build-selfcheck.json` for the ACS toolchain gate. The advisory binding skips its duplicate run when the floor is enforced, so one build has one go-test pass.
    - **The floor package set** is built by `floorTestPackages`. It takes the changed Go packages and adds the owner package of each changed non-Go file under `go/`, found with `changedpkgs.PatternOf`. Thus a lane that changes only a fixture or an embedded file runs the tests of that package. Changes to `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `vendor/` and the module root are left to the composed gates and CI. The cover-changed floor, the lint floor and the covering-tests artifact keep `changedGoTestPackages`, because a data file has no statements. The repo-contract data rows select data that is read across packages, so the two rules do not overlap; the advisory `buildSelfCheck` stays Go-only.
  - The floor reads `go/.apicover-enforce` and `go/.cover-strict` from the base and from the tree together (`baseAndTreeList`, `floor_lists_base.go`, 2026-10-21). `floorListRegressions` (`floor_lists_regression.go`) refuses a lane that drops a base line or lowers a base floor. It also refuses a `.cover-strict` line that does not parse. An unreadable base WARNs, and the floor judges the tree list alone (gate-input-inventory D3). (TestEnforcedPackages_ABuildCannotDropAPackageFromTheBaseList, TestCoverChangedFailures_TheStrictFloorsOfTheBaseStillBindAfterTheBuildLowersThem, TestFloorListRegressions_RefusesADroppedOrLoweredLineAndAcceptsARaise)
  - **Floor parity** (`build_floor_retake.go`, test-system §4.5). `floorScopedPackages` applies `ciparity.TierScope` before the unit run. Thus the floor does not run an env-exclusive package under a live loop, the same as the audit tier. It writes a NOTE with the backstop.
  - `retakeFloorRun` retakes one red run once, under the integration-tier lock (`flock.LockWithin` on `ciparity.TierLockPath`). A lock that is not available runs the retake unserialized, with a WARN. The unit run (`retakeSelfCheckFailures`), each added tag-gated package and the coverage run (`coverPlan.retake` in `scopedCoverProfile`) use it.
  - `emitFloorRetake` emits WARN `BUILD_FLOOR_RETAKE_GREEN` or `BUILD_FLOOR_RETAKE_RED` with `fields.pkg`, `fields.tests` and `fields.retake`. It writes the same line to stderr. `floorFailureDiagnostic` keeps the lines of `goTestFailureLines` first, then the tail, in the 400-byte bound. These lines are each `--- FAIL:` line and each `FAIL <package>` line. Pinned by `TestChangedPackageFloorChecks_AOneOffRedIsRetakenAndTheBuildGoesOn`, `TestChangedPackageFloorChecks_ASecondRedFailsTheFloorAndNamesTheTest`, `TestScopedCoverProfile_AOneOffRedIsRetakenUnderTheTierLock`, `TestFloorFailureDiagnostic_KeepsTheTestNameBefore10KBOfLaterOutput` and the journey `TestBuildFloorJourney_Cycle2151_AOneOffRedIsRetakenAndTheBuildGoesOn`.
  - **A new test gets no retake** (`build_floor_newtests.go`, 2026-11-14). The floor of cycle 2353 ran a new test red twice. Each retake was green, so the build went on. Ship ran the same package once and refused it.
  - `DefaultBuildFloorChecks` reads the changed files and the `addedtests.Groups` result one time (`withFloorAdded`). This is the derivation that ship uses. `retakeFloorOutcome` takes the packages that its run covered. A green retake is `floorRetakeNewTestRed` when a `FAIL` line of the first run names a covered package with an added test.
  - That outcome emits WARN `BUILD_FLOOR_FLAKY_NEW_TEST` with `fields.pkg`, `fields.tests`, `fields.retake=green` and `fields.class=flaky_new_test`. The plain run, the tagged run and the cover run (`coverPlan.retake`) all honor it. A package without an added test keeps `BUILD_FLOOR_RETAKE_GREEN`.
  - The correction is the first line of the failure output (`attachCorrection`). `floorFailureDiagnostic` and the enforced renderer cut only the text after it (`splitCorrection`). A long red keeps the test name and the reason.
  - If the changed files cannot be read, the floor keeps the old rule and writes a WARN. If no base SHA is known, it keeps the old rule and writes no WARN.
  - Pinned by `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_AGreenRetakeDoesNotClearATestTheCycleAdded`, `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_AnInheritedTestKeepsTheGreenRetake`, `TestScopedCoverProfile_AGreenRetakeOfANewTestFailsTheCoverPath`, `TestFloorAdded_NewTestRedsByCallerShape`, `TestAddedTaggedTestFailures_AGreenRetakeOfANewTaggedTestIsRed`, `TestUnitTestFloorJudge_AGreenRetakeOfANewTestIsRed` and `TestCoverChangedFloorJudge_AGreenRetakeOfANewTestIsRed`.
  - **Floor convergence** (`floor_convergence.go`, test-system D4.5.4). `floorConvergence.observe` keeps the findings of the last two floor rejections. `floorFindingsConverge` is true when the later set has fewer findings, or the same number with a finding that the earlier set lacks. Findings compare after `normalizeReasonForFingerprint`, and `grant` gives one further correction, up to `RetryConfig.FloorConvergenceRounds`, and emits INFO `BUILD_FLOOR_CONVERGING`. `cycleRun.raiseOrGrant` and `resumedCorrector.correct` ask the retry ladder first and grant only when it does not raise. Only the Build phase has a bound above 0.
  - `resumedRejection` marks a rejection of the resume gate. `resume_execution.go` records a coded FAIL (`rejectedOutcome`) for it. The single helper `failPhase` records the outcome and calls `recordFailureLearning`, so the digest and the retrospective see a resumed rejection. The same helper serves the typed end, the dispatch error, the non-canonical verdict, the untyped review error and the Build explanation refresh abort. The no-runner end and the ledger-append end only record the outcome, as the fresh path does. See [checkpoint and resume](../checkpoint-resume.md).
  - `addedTaggedTestFailures` runs every added test package under its declared build tags.
    - It uses the same seed (`changedpkgs.ChangedFilesChecked`) and grouping (`addedtests.Groups`) as ship's added-test backstop.
    - Each package runs under `addedtests.PackageTimeout` (20 min), which `repoContractTestTimeout` aliases.
    - Thus the floor is never stricter than a later gate running that package (`taggedTestArgs`, 2026-09-30).
    - At 120 s the floor killed cycle 1763's `-count=50 -race` predicate, and the lane edited the floor to survive.
  - **The floor runs in two stages** (`build_handoff_floor.go`, plan S6). Each `BuildFloorCheck` has `Run` for Stage A and optional `Tests` for Stage B.
    - Stage A holds cheap static checks: protected surface, explanation documentation, removal claims, placeholder tokens, persona budget and docs lint. It also checks comment floor, size floor, floor lists, scope delta, gate classifier and lint. The derived files check joins this list in `productionStaticFloorChecks`. `StaticBuildFloorChecks` holds the engine part.
    - `BuildHandoffFloor.Failures` runs Stage A and reports all failures together. It runs Stage B (`SuiteBuildFloorChecks` and the repo-contract pack) only when Stage A is green.
    - `BuildHandoffFloor.StaticFailures` runs Stage A only so an explanation gap skips long tests.
    - `DefaultBuildFloorChecks` keeps the same order for direct calls: static first, then suite.
    - Pinned by `TestBuildFloor_StageAReportsAllStaticFailuresTogether`, `TestBuildFloor_AStageARedSkipsTheTestRun`, `TestBuildFloor_AGreenStageARunsTheTestsAfterEveryStaticCheck`, `TestBuildHandoffFloor_StaticFailuresNeverRunTheTests`, `TestMandatoryExplanationReviewer_AnExplanationGapSkipsTheComposedTests` and `TestBuildFloorStages_TheDefaultCheckRunsTheTestsOnlyAfterAGreenStaticStage`.

  **One go-test budget for every floor run (2026-10-05).**
  - `unitTestArgs` and `coverTestArgs` run under `addedtests.PackageTimeout` too, so all floor `go test` calls and ship's repo contract share one deadline.
  - Before, the self-check used `-timeout 120s` and the coverage pass used `-timeout 300s`.
  - A green `./cmd/evolve` takes 105–127 s on the loop host, so the floor killed cycles 1787, 1791, 1792 and 1798.
  - A floor deadline is the wrong alarm for slow packages; a package wall-time budget check belongs in `cmd-evolve-unit-wall-time-and-package-budget-check`.
  - `TestGoTestTimeouts_AreTheSharedPackageBudget` (`internal/guards`) fails any `-timeout` or `-test.timeout` in non-test code under `go/internal` or `go/cmd` whose value is not `addedtests.PackageTimeout`.
  - A go-test argv with no `-timeout` uses Go's 10 m default; `phase_bindings_selfcheck.go` and `addedtests/budget.go` stay on the protected surface.
  - **Hold time and wait time are separate budgets.** The coverage pass holds `verifylock` while it runs, up to the 20 m budget.
  - A lane queued behind a hung holder waits at most `verifylock.MaxWait` (15 min, `coverLockWait`), then runs unserialized with a WARN (`verifylock.AcquireWithin`).
  - Pinned by `TestScopedCoverFunc_BehindAHeldLockWaitsOnlyItsBoundThenRunsUnserialized` and `TestCoverLockWait_IsTheSharedVerificationWaitBound`.
  - `realGoUnitTest` takes no lock, and a sandboxed lane pane cannot open the lock file (`verifylock-unreachable-from-sandboxed-pane`).
  - Pinned also by `TestUnitTestArgs_RunAChangedPackageUnderTheSharedBudget`, `TestCoverTestArgs_RunTheCoveragePassUnderTheSharedBudget` and `TestTaggedTestArgs_RunAnAddedPackageUnderShipsBackstopBudget`.

  The changed-path set is derived once per handoff (`changedFloorPaths`) and passed down, so path-driven engines judge one diff with one `git diff`.
- **Protected-surface floor** (`ProtectedSurfaceFloorChecks`). It asks the ship tripwire's question (`ship/integrity.go` `verifyNoControlPlaneEdits`) at the one phase that can act on it. The membership predicate (`guards.IsProtectedSurface`) is injected by the composition root because `guards` imports `core`. The floor holds a shell-written protected file at `shadow` and `enforce` when `workflow.build_floor` is on; ship holds it when off (`TestAShellWrittenProtectedFileIsHeldByTheBuildFloorAndTheShipTripwire`, [llm-isolation.md](../llm-isolation.md) §9.3).
- **Gate classifier floor** (`GateClassifierFloorChecks`, X15). It gives the build diff since the cycle base and untracked paths to `gateclass.Classify`. Each flag is one floor failure and emits `HEALING_I10_VIOLATION`. A git error fails the floor. It passes no protected predicate because the protected-surface floor reports those paths ([internal-gateclass.md](internal-gateclass.md)).
- **Scope-delta floor** (`ScopeDeltaFloorChecks`, `scope_delta_floor.go`, [ADR-0087](../adr/0087-scope-delta-adjudication.md)). At build exit it classifies each changed path against the lane's scope with `scopedelta.Classify`. It emits `ORCHESTRATOR_SCOPE_DELTA` and writes one stderr line per out-of-scope path without blocking.
  - `LaneDeclaredFiles` reads `files` from `.evolve/inbox/processing/cycle-N/`, filtered by `committedset`. `SiblingLaneFiles` finds sibling `runs/cycle-*` lanes sharing `goal_hash` (`cross-lane`).
  - `ClassifyLaneChanges` (`lane_scope_assembly.go`) builds `scopedelta.Scope` and runs `Classify` on changed paths, shared with `evolve cycle scope-delta`.
  - An unreadable claim file errors rather than returning a partial list (`TestLaneDeclaredFiles_AnUnreadableClaimFileRefusesThePartialScope`).
  - `guards.IsProtectedSurface` is injected by the composition root and marks paths as `boundary`.
  - The signal is `gate.passed`: WARN for carve and refuse, INFO for computed closure, sent to `ReviewInput.Signals`.
  - Cycles without declared files, worktree, or project root report nothing; unreadable claim dirs WARN and unreadable pins drop only sibling detection.
  - Pinned by `TestScopeDeltaFloorChecks_ReportsEveryOutOfScopePathWithOneCodedSignal`, `TestScopeDeltaFloorChecks_IsSilentWithoutAScopeToCompareAgainst`, `TestScopeDeltaFloorChecks_AnUnreadablePinLosesTheWaveNotTheReport`, `TestClassifyLaneChanges_JudgesEveryChangedPathAgainstOneAssembledScope`, `TestSiblingLaneFiles_SameWaveOnlyAndWarnsOnACorruptPin`, `TestLaneDeclaredFiles_FollowsThePinAndCountsEveryClaimWithoutOne`, `TestReviewInputFor_TheOrchestratorHandsItsSignalCenterToTheFloor` and `TestProductionBuildFloorChecks_ReportsAScopeDeltaThroughTheComposedFloor`.
- **Repo-contract floor** (`RepoContractFloorChecks`, `repo_contract_floor.go`).
  - It runs `repocontract.Packages()` at build exit so lane-caused reds return to build correction before audit (cycle 1745).
  - `RepoContractPackFn` is injected by the composition root as `ship.RunRepoContractPack` because `phases/ship` imports `core`.
  - `repocontract.PackRuns(gate, root)` gates execution on `gates.repo_contract_gate` (default `enforce`; unset/`off` disable; typos enforce) and `<root>/go/go.mod` declaring `github.com/mickeyyaya/evolve-loop/go`.
  - Foreign modules (such as #711 `e2e.local/fixture`) skip; deleted guard suites fail red (`pattern …: no such file or directory`).
  - Unreadable or unparseable policies WARN and run the pack.
- **The project root of the repo-contract floor.** `RepoContractPackFn` takes project root and worktree; passing `ReviewInput.ProjectRoot` writes greens to the shared verdict store ([internal-testverdict.md](internal-testverdict.md), `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_HandsThePackTheProjectRootOfTheSharedVerdictStore`).
- **The pack wall time in the floor WARN.** `RepoContractFloorJudge` measures wall time; `unnamedPackExit` logs it beside the floor deadline (`TestUnnamedPackExit_NamesThePackWallTimeAndTheFloorDeadline`, `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_AnUnnamedExitWarnsWithTheWallTimeOfItsRun`).
- **The changed paths of the repo-contract floor.** Plan: `docs/plans/data-file-test-floor-2026-11.md` (PR 1514).
  - `RepoContractPackFn` receives changed paths from `floorPaths` so data-only edits (such as `.github/workflows/required.yml`) run matching data rows.
  - Unreadable paths WARN and pass `repocontract.UnknownChange()` to run the coarse set.
  - Pinned by `TestRepoContractFloorJudge_AWorkflowOnlyWorktreeRunsTheTestsThatReadIt`, `TestRepoContractFloorJudge_AGoOnlyWorktreeHandsThePackOnlyGoPaths` and `TestRepoContractFloorJudge_AnUnreadableChangeRunsTheCoarseSet`.
- **Build explanation floor.**
  - `MandatoryBuildHandoffFloor()` runs in the mandatory reviewer outside optional seams so callers cannot replace it.
  - `NewExplanationLifecycleReviewer` runs last in the production chain and seals Build output only after earlier reviewers approve.
  - `guards/integrity_surface_explanation_callsites_test.go` parses lifecycle vocabulary from function declarations in `build_explanation_handoff.go`.
- **One named floor, run by the cycle and by both pre-handoff probes** (`build_handoff_floor.go`, [ADR-0117](../adr/0117-pre-handoff-probes-run-the-build-floor.md)).
  - `BuildHandoffFloor` is an ordered list of `BuildFloorCheck{Name, Run}` whose `Failures` runs all checks and `Review` rejects build handoffs with `Retry` set.
  - Core owns `MandatoryBuildHandoffFloor()` (explanation-documentation check); the composition root owns composed checks and mounts `WholeBuildHandoffFloor(composed)`.
  - `WholeBuildHandoffFloor` is what `evolve selfcheck build` and `evolve phase verify build` run.
  - `Orchestrator.BuildHandoffFloorNames()` walks the mandatory half and every `BuildHandoffFloor` in the chain.
  - When the explanation half rejects, the mandatory reviewer also runs `floorChecks` and returns one union of failures in check order.
  - Other reviewers do not run on a floor rejection (`TestMandatoryExplanationReviewer_AnExplanationGapCarriesEveryComposedFloorFailureInOneRejection`).
- **The TDD handoff floor** (`tdd_predicate_floor.go`, [incident 2026-11-09](../../incidents/2026-11-09-duplicate-ratchet-misses-staged-predicates.md)). `withMandatoryReviewers` puts `tddPredicateFloor` before the mandatory explanation reviewer. Every orchestrator gets it through `WithReviewer` or `NewOrchestrator`.
  - On a TDD handoff it scans `go/` of the worktree with `internal/testdup`. It rejects with retry each group of equal helper bodies that has a site under `go/acs/cycle<N>/`. No phase after TDD can change that directory, so the copy must leave there.
  - The correction names the replacement of each copy. `testdup.Group.Wraps` lists the `acsassert` functions that the copy calls. The floor reads `TestingForms` and the `testing.T` functions from `go/pkg/acsassert` of the worktree, so no second list exists.
  - If every site is in `go/acs/cycle<N>/`, the text says to call the existing helper. If any site is elsewhere, the text says that a test helper of another package cannot be imported. It then does one of four things:
    - It names the kit function with the same body (for example `acsassert.ExitedPID`).
    - It names the `testing.T` form of a kit call (for example `acsassert.GoModuleDir`).
    - It says to inline the copy or to call a `testing.T` kit function that it wraps.
    - It says to write the statements inside the test function and define no helper function, because a renamed helper is still a copy.
  - The floor finds the kit function with the same body when it loads the kit. `testdup.NormalizedBodies` gives the normalized body of each kit function that takes a `testing.T`, and the floor compares it with `Group.Body`. No second list exists.
  - A tree without `go/.test-dup-baseline` is not judged. A baseline or a test file that the floor cannot read gives a `[tdd-floor] WARN` and an approval; the ship ratchet still counts the copies.
- **The floor runs before the build session closes** (`builder_selfcheck.go`, `builder_selfcheck_receipt.go`, [ADR-0158](../adr/0158-a-repair-round-runs-only-for-a-finding-the-lane-can-fix-and-the-auditor-applies-a-small-prescribed-fix.md) decision 18).
  - Problem: a floor refusal at the review gate costs a new build dispatch with the full prompt. Wave 148 cycles 2092, 2093 and 2095 failed at the gate after their one correction, on cover-changed and Changed Areas findings.
  - `WriteSelfCheckReceipt` writes `builder-selfcheck-receipt.json` in the workspace. The receipt holds the tree digest of the worktree (`treefence.Take`, which includes untracked files) and the GREEN result. `evolve selfcheck build` and `evolve phase verify build` write it when the check binds the cycle.
  - `selfCheckReceiptState` names the receipt for the current tree: `green`, `red`, `stale` (another tree, or a tree that cannot be read) or `absent`.
  - Only at `enforce`, `cycleRun.builderSelfCheckBeforeClose` gives a build dispatch a `CloseCheck` in `PhaseRequest.BeforeClose`. It gives none when the reviewer chain has no floor.
  - The check uses the floor list of the reviewer chain (`floorChecks`), so it runs the same checks as the review gate. A GREEN receipt for the current tree skips the floor. The check returns the findings message and a report function.
  - The bridge calls the report after the paste. The report writes `builder-selfcheck-findings.json` and records `BUILD_SELFCHECK_FINDINGS` with `source: bridge` and `decision: delivered`, or `delivery_failed` and the error. Thus the record says what happened.
  - At `shadow`, the wait runs no floor, so shadow adds no wall time. `cycleRun.recordShadowSelfCheck` reads the first review of the build after the close. When the gate rejects on the floor and the receipt is not `green`, it records the gate findings with `source: gate` and `decision: would_return_in_session`. It runs no second floor.
  - The review gate runs the floor again after the session closes. Thus the check changes when the findings reach the builder, not what the gate accepts.
- **The floor's inputs have one projection.**
  - `ReviewInputFor(cs, phase, projectRoot)` turns cycle bindings into a `ReviewInput`.
  - Both dispatch ladders read the worktree from that input, and resume sets `cycle_id` on legacy checkpoints once.
  - `ReadRunCycleState(workspace, evolveDir)` reads the run's `run.json` mirror first, else `ResolveCycleStatePath(evolveDir)`.
  - Missing or unparseable files return an error naming the path, never a zero state.
  - `BuildHandoffProbe{Workspace, EvolveDir, ProjectRoot, Worktree}.Input()` refuses mismatched worktrees and falls back to unbound inputs with a reason.
- **One fresh session after a session-recoverable end** (`launch_recoverable.go`, [ADR-0044 amendment of 2026-10-11](../adr/0044-unified-phase-recovery-protocol.md)). `LaunchRecoverable(ctx, launch, req, onRecoverable)` is the one retry loop of F31.
  - It launches once. When the response is `SessionRecoverable` and the context is live, it asks `onRecoverable` for the next request, marks it `FreshSession` and launches once more.
  - It clears `SessionRecoverable` on the second response, so a caller around it does not retry again.
  - `LaunchWithFreshSession(ctx, bridge, req)` uses `SameRequest(req)` as the hook. The phase runner passes its postmortem hook.
  - The phase advisor, the plan judge, the failure advisor and the retry adjudicator use `LaunchWithFreshSession`. Retro, the swarm workers, `subagent` and the chain walk of `bridgechain` use it too.
  - Pinned by `TestLaunchRecoverable_*` and one `…SurvivesADeadShellFirstSession` test for each caller.
- **Advisor seams kept in core.**
  - `AgentIdentity` aliases `advisor.Identity`, so failure advisor and retry adjudicator keep their spelling.
  - `AdvisorDepthExceeded` returns false (`EVOLVE_ADVISOR_DEPTH` is retired; the mint denylist is the recursion guard, [04-advisor.md](../decomposition/04-advisor.md)).
- **ShipError has no code-to-class mapper in core.** Each call site in `internal/phases/ship` chooses the class, and `NewShipError` records it verbatim. The table in `apicover_shiperror_test.go` is transcribed from those call sites, so a drift between the table and production is a protocol regression.
- **Test-side fixtures.** `advisor_fixture_test.go` holds the one rich `RouteInput` the advisor goldens are captured over, rendering every prompt section at once. `apicover_*_test.go` files name and exercise exported symbols for the `apicover` gate ([ADR-0050](../adr/0050-modularization-and-unified-phase-io.md)). Each asserts a real behavior, never `_ = pkg.X` padding.
- **The handoff floor's text checks** (`build_removal_check.go`, `build_placeholder_check.go`, `build_persona_budget_check.go`).
  - A removal claim is parsed only from a fenced ```` ```json ```` block with `removedPaths` in the build report or `deliverables/`.
  - Each claimed path is checked against the worktree and the Git index (`git ls-files --error-unmatch`).
  - `PlaceholderTokenFailures` refuses any `UPPER_SNAKE_PLACEHOLDER` slot left in the report, one failure per token per line.
  - The persona budget check runs `./internal/prompts` when the diff touches `agents/evolve-*.md` outside the derived set.
- **Build-leak recovery** (`recoverBuildLeak`, in `buildleak_recover_test.go` and `leak_ownership_recovery_test.go`).
  - Non-Claude builders can write into the main tree; recovery moves leaks into the worktree and stages them for `git diff HEAD`.
  - Tests use `git worktree add` because two-repo fixtures masked directory-rename bugs.
  - **The parameter objects.** `leakRecovery` carries root, worktree, cycle, baseline, `writeAuthority`, `mainTreeOwnership` and `exempt`.
  - `Orchestrator.recoverPhaseLeak` builds it from `phaseLeakScope` for both live and crash-resume paths.
  - Source writers author all paths, scout authors its lane evals, and other phases relocate only untracked non-deliverables.
  - **The `-uall` listing.** Untracked files come one per line, so `os.Rename` avoids directory collisions, and nested worktrees skip through `isLegitimateMainTreePath`.
  - **A worktree copy is never overwritten, and a differing main-tree copy is never lost.**
    - Worktree copies win; identical main-tree copies are dropped.
    - Differing main-tree copies move to `quarantineDir(projectRoot, cycle)` under `uniqueQuarantinePath` with a WARN.
    - Unreadable copies fail recovery and keep the main copy.
    - Pinned by `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ADifferingMainTreeCopyIsQuarantinedNotDeleted`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AnIdenticalMainTreeCopyIsDroppedWithoutQuarantine` and `TestRecoverBeforeReview_OwnEvalLeakNeverOverwritesTheWorktreeCopy`.
  - **Fail, never report clean.**
    - Main-tree reads retry through `withSnapshotRetries`; failing reads or unrecoverable leaks return false and fail the phase.
    - `git add -f` stages relocated paths (needed for gitignored files); a stage failure returns false.
- **Main-tree ownership** (`leak_ownership.go`). N fleet lanes share one main tree, so "absent from my baseline" no longer means "mine". `mainTreeOwnership` is the one Specification that answers "may this lane claim this main-tree path?" Five owner rules key a path to its owner:
  - `.evolve/evals/<slug>.md` to the item `<slug>`;
  - `go/acs/cycle<N>/…` (through `acssuite.AncestorCyclePackages`) and `docs/explain/builds/cycle-<N>-<run>.md` (through `explanationdocs.IsCycleChangeRecord`) to cycle N;
  - a verified active mint to the mint registry;
  - the dossier `knowledge-base/cycles/cycle-<N>.json` or `.md` to the dossier of cycle N (2026-10-17).

  A lane holds three things:
  - the items that `committedset.Committed` binds it to (the lane pin, else the top_n of triage, minus deferrals);
  - the slugs that it materialized in its workspace eval home;
  - its own cycle number.

  With no pin, no decision and no materialized eval, and with no cycle number, the lane holds everything, which is the single-writer rule.
  - **Recovery reads `foreignOwner`, whatever the path's git status, and fails closed when nobody live holds the owner.** A path keyed to an owner the lane does not hold is never claimed.
    - When a live sibling or the mint registry holds the owner (`heldBySibling`), the path stays with one line (`left <path> in the main tree — it belongs to <owner>, not this lane`).
    - A real cycle's dossier has an owner when its run directory exists; recovery leaves it, while unknown or self dossiers stay leaks.
    - Sibling ships write dossiers while this lane runs (`TestFilterRealLeaks_AnotherCyclesDossierIsNotThisLanesLeakButItsOwnIs`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AShippedCyclesDossierStaysInTheMainTreeAndIsNoLeak`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ThisCyclesOwnDossierInTheMainTreeIsStillALeak`).
    - When nobody live holds it, recovery fails the phase with a WARN naming path and owner; expired sibling leases hold nothing.
    - Recovery runs on both entry points (`TestRecoverBeforeReview_AKeyedPathNoLiveLaneHoldsFailsRecovery`, `TestRecoveryFailsAPhaseThatLeftAnEvalNoLiveLaneHolds`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AnEditToAnUnownedCommittedEvalFailsTheResume`).
    - Siblings' staged changes to their own committed evals stay untouched before the status switch (`TestRecoverBeforeReview_SiblingsStagedChangesToItsCommittedEvalAreLeft`).
    - Trade-off: an own leaked edit is left if a live sibling holds it; if none holds it, recovery fails closed.
  - **Recovery and the guard read one exemption set, `leakExemptions`.** It is built by `mainTreeOwnership.exemptions` from ownership and console lease, then passed in `phaseLeakScope`.
    - Keyed unowned paths are exempt only when a live sibling holds the owner (`liveSiblingsOf`) or a registered mint holds it.
    - A console-leased path is exempt. Recovery leaves it with one line, and the guard waives it with its WARN.
    - A key nobody live holds is still charged to the phase, so the guard is never weaker than before.
    - An unkeyed path, such as a source file, can have an owner too. The owner is a live sibling of the same goal that names the exact file in its scope, when this lane does not hold it. This closed the inbox item `treediff-guard-worktree-phase-crosslane-residual` (2026-10-20).
    - Only an exact-file entry counts. A directory entry, such as `go/internal/core`, holds nothing. Real items declare directories often, and a prefix match excused this lane's own leaks under them (independent review of PR #1037).
    - A sibling counts only when its lane pin has the goal hash of this lane's pin, the sibling rule of `SiblingLaneFiles`. A lane pin without a goal hash gives no exemption.
    - This lane holds a path that its own declared scope or a closure rule covers. `liveSiblings.declaresAgainst` asks `scopedelta.Classify` with `DefaultClosureRules`, so the scope rule has one home.
    - The guard does not charge such a path. It WARNs with the path and the holder (`excused: the declared scope of cycle N holds it`). Recovery leaves the path in the main tree with one line. The guard of the sibling charges it.
    - The trade-off: the sibling's file can be a leak of this lane. Then the sibling's guard charges it only inside its own phase window.
    - A lane with an unreadable or empty scope holds every unkeyed path. A sibling with an unreadable scope holds none. A WARN names each unreadable scope, so an unknown scope never makes the guard weaker.
    - A protected surface is never exempt, and with no protected-surface rule (`WithProtectedSurface`) no unkeyed path is exempt. The production orchestrator always has the rule.
    - Pinned by `TestFilterRealLeaks_OnlyAFileALiveSameGoalSiblingNamesIsNotThisLanesLeak`, `TestFilterRealLeaks_ALaneWithoutAKnownScopeOrSurfaceIsChargedForEveryUnkeyedPath`, `TestRecoverPath_LeavesAPathAnotherLaneHoldsAndNamesTheHolder`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ASourceALiveSiblingDeclaresStaysInTheMainTree`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ASiblingsDirectoryEntryDoesNotHoldThisLanesLeak` and `TestGuard_APathALiveSiblingDeclaresDoesNotAbortThisLanesPhase`.
    - The registry is one of the owner rules, so `filterRealLeaks` has no second copy of the mint check.
    - Crash-resume adopts no console lease, so a leased keyed path no live lane holds fails a resume.
    - Pinned by `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AConsoleLeasedPathIsLeftJustAsTheGuardWaivesIt`.
  - **The eval layout has one home.** `EvalFilePath(root, slug)` builds `<root>/.evolve/evals/<slug>.md`; recovery, ownership and `evalgate` all use it.
- **The recovery baseline is captured per phase, and the dispatch carries it.**
  - `cycleRun.snapshotMainTree` takes one `git status --porcelain -uall` per dispatch into `dispatchResult.beforeDirty`.
  - Recovery and the tree-diff guard read that one set at every dispatch, remediation fix, re-run, and resume.
  - **A failed snapshot is retried, and a phase never runs without one.**
    - Every main-tree read goes through `withSnapshotRetries`: the guard's pre- and post-phase reads (`retryingDirtyPaths`) and recovery's own `git status` (`readMainTreeStatus`).
      - It makes 3 tries with 100 ms then 200 ms backoff through `backoffSleep`, and stops when cancelled.
      - Recovery that still cannot read fails the phase instead of reporting the tree clean.
      - The guard aborts the phase when its post-phase read, or its re-check after the binary discard, still fails (`abortUncheckedPhase`).
      - The post-phase check runs before the ledger append, so a leak or an unreadable tree aborts the cycle without recording success.
    - `defaultGitDirtyPaths` returns git's error instead of an empty set.
    - When every try fails in a git checkout, `snapshotMainTree` returns `errPrePhaseSnapshot` and skips the phase.
    - Fresh dispatches abort with that error, remediation rounds void their fix, and resumed dispatches fail resume (`cycleRun.snapshotThenDispatch`).
    - Only a root without `.git` (`plane.Classify` not-exist) skips the guard; an unreadable `.git` is treated as a checkout.
    - The checkout decision runs once per cycle (`cycleRun.mainTreeIsCheckout`, shared through `resumeExecution.checkoutDecision`) so deleting `.git` cannot disable guards.
    - The earlier rule, which degraded to "guard and recovery skipped", let a leak made during a transient failure ship silently.
    - Pinned by `TestRunCycle_ATransientPrePhaseSnapshotFailureIsRetriedAndTheLeakRecovered`, `TestRunCycle_APrePhaseSnapshotThatKeepsFailingStopsBeforeThePhaseRuns`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_APrePhaseSnapshotThatKeepsFailingStopsBeforeThePhaseRuns`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AFailedSnapshotNeverRecoversAgainstAnEmptyBaseline`, `TestDefaultGitDirtyPaths_ReportsAFailedGitStatus`, `TestCycleRun_RecoveryBaselineNeedsASnapshotOfACheckout` and `TestRecoverPhaseLeak_SkipsAPhaseWithNoPrePhaseSnapshot`.
  - **The remediation round's observer spans the whole run.**
    - `cycleRun.snapshotThenRun` takes the snapshot, then `observedRun` starts the observer across the phase (`TestObservedRun_TheObserverWatchesTheWholeRun`, `TestMaybeRemediate_TheGateReRunsOwnLeakIsRecovered`).
    - Dirt present at phase start is never that phase's leak; later phases no longer sweep earlier residue unless it must not move.
- **Carry-forward filter** (`carryforward_filter.go`). A deterministic, zero-LLM screen. `CarryforwardCandidateLandable` runs an is-ancestor and `git cherry` patch-id supersession screen, then a real 3-way `git merge-tree --write-tree` dry run, which writes only to the object DB. `ClassifyFleetRebaseCandidate` splits a not-landable answer into already-landed and conflict for ship recovery's fleet-rebase pre-screen (`recoverFromShipError`); `evolve branches audit|prune` uses the same screen. All git goes through the `gitRunner` seam, which `carryforward_filter_test.go` scripts per subcommand (`seamGit`).
- **The fleet rebase with derived regeneration** (`ship_recovery.go`, [ADR-0049](../adr/0049-concurrent-multi-cycle-execution.md)). `rebaseCycleBranchOntoMain` rebases the cycle branch onto `main` after a peer moved `main`.
  - It never rebases the project root, and an empty worktree does not rebase.
  - The result is `ok` for a clean replay. It is also `ok` when each conflict is a derived conflict of the `internal/derived` catalog. Then the rebase regenerates each fired entry once, with the generator of the worktree.
  - The re-audit binds the regenerated tree, so the tree binding of ship still holds.
  - A conflict on any other path returns those paths to the debugger. An infra failure or a failed regeneration returns no paths.
  - The abort runs under a detached context with a 30 s limit. Thus a cancelled cycle context cannot leave the worktree half rebased.
  - A replayed commit that becomes empty after the regeneration is skipped.
  - `rebase --continue` runs with `core.editor=true`, so it keeps the replayed message and does not wait for an editor.
  - A `--continue` that fails means that a later commit conflicts. The loop classifies it again, up to `maxRebaseContinueSteps`.
  - `unmergedRebasePaths` reads `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U -z`. Thus each path has the same bytes as the paths that the worktree fence reports.
- **The inbox stamp of the cycle's own item** (`ship_rebase_inbox.go`). The plane commits `chore(inbox)` stamps to an inbox item on `main`, and the cycle can change or consume the same item. The two edits conflict in the fleet rebase. Before the rebase decides that a path is a real conflict, `inboxStamps.resolve` settles each conflicted file under `.evolve/inbox/` that holds an item of the cycle. `LaneScopeIDs` of the workspace gives the item ids. A fixed rule applies:
  - If the cycle consumed the item, the file is removed. The consumed copy stays. The cycle's consumption wins only when the index holds the matching `consumed/` copy. A deletion with no consumed copy stays a conflict.
  - If the cycle changed the item, `mergeInboxItem` merges the JSON field by field. A field that the cycle changed lands. A field that `main` changed stays. When both changed one field, the value of `main` stays.
  - The merged item must keep the `id` of the item. A `null` item, an item with no `id` and an item with another `id` stay conflicts.
  - The merged file has compact JSON with sorted keys and no final newline. A plane writer with another layout shows a whole-file diff on `main`. This is accepted.
  - A conflict on any other path, or on the inbox file of another item, keeps the old result: the rebase aborts and names the path. An empty `id` in the scope owns no item.
  - A failed git call is never a merge fact. The stage list comes from `git ls-files -u -z`.
  - If the list, a stage read, a write or `git add` fails, the rebase aborts and the lane is restored. The result is `ok=false`, with no stamped path and no resolved signal.
  - The replay is never skipped while the index holds a staged change. A `--continue` that fails after a resolve aborts the rebase and keeps the audited commit.
  - After a resolve, `auditedDrift` proves that the lane keeps its audited code. It merges `main` with the audited commit through `git merge-tree --write-tree`. It then lists the paths where the rebased `HEAD` differs from that merge tree. The list excludes `.evolve/inbox/` and the outputs of the derived entries. A peer that landed the same file, or that edited lines near the lane, gives an empty list, so a correct rebase lands. Code that the rebase lost or changed gives a non-empty list.
  - If the list is not empty, the lane is reset to the audited commit and the result is `ok=false`. The conflicts are the listed paths and the stamped inbox paths, so the recovery routes to the debugger. If `git merge-tree` or the diff fails, the lane is reset and the result is `ok=false` with no conflict.
  - `abort` clears the stamped paths. A path appears once in the record, also when several lane commits change the item.
  - The resolve writes only inbox files. The tree of the code is the same before and after, so the audit binding of the code tree holds.
  - `rebaseReport.run` is the one place that runs the rebase and publishes `ORCHESTRATOR_FLEET_REBASE_INBOX_RESOLVED` with `fields.paths`. The ship recovery, the re-entry after the debugger and `ReauditCycle` all call it. Each route records the paths in `CycleState.ShipRecoveryStamped`. `ReauditInput.Signals` gives `ReauditCycle` the signal center.
  - A fail reason names `GIT_FLEET_REBASE_CONFLICT` when the rebase met a conflict (`recoveryReasonCode`). The reason of the ship recovery and the reason of `endAfterDebugger` use it. The digest class is then `ship-rebase-conflict` (`classifyPreClass`). Before, the reason named `GIT_FLEET_REBASE_NEEDED` and the class was `unknown`.
