- **A judgment lesson dedupes by todo id across re-dispatch within a cycle.** Both fresh and resumed paths call `recordJudgmentLesson` beside `recordFloorVerdictFailure` (`TestJudgmentLesson_DedupedByID`, `TestJudgmentLesson_WiredInLockstepWithFloorRecorder`).
- **Every catalog phase that declares `classify.verdict_from_sentinel` must be in `judgmentTeachingPhases`.** This applies at declaration time to all git-tracked catalog phases (`TestJudgmentTeachingPhases_CoverEveryPhaseThatCanStateItsOwnVerdict`).
- **Every dispatched phase runs with cwd=worktree; `worktreePhase` gates the WRITE axis only.** Read-only checks inspect pending worktree files while write gates stay scoped to source writers (`TestCB1_EveryDispatchedPhaseCarriesWorktree`, `TestWorktreePhase_WriteAxisIsSourceWritersOnly`).
- **The advisor sees CLI health before it plans.** `benchedCLIsForRouting` projects active cli-health benches into `RouteInput` so planners skip quota-walled families (`TestBenchedCLIsForRouting_ProjectsActiveSorted`).
- **The recursion-guard denylist covers `router`, `evolve-router`, `advisor`, and `failure-advisor` (case-insensitive).** Reserved mint names drop with a reason (`TestMintConfigsFrom_RejectsAdvisorRoleMint`).
- **The advisor's bridge launch must carry the cycle's git worktree.** `RouteInput.ActiveWorktree` flows from `cs.ActiveWorktree` so tmux drivers under `EVOLVE_FLEET=1` never fail (`TestAdvisorLaunch_ThreadsActiveWorktree`, `TestAdvisorPlanInput_ThreadsActiveWorktree`).
- **The Plan prompt instructs the agent to write the absolute `routing-plan.json` path.** Under claude-tmux, REPL cwd varies, so the prompt path must match `advisorLaunch`'s `ArtifactPath` (`TestPhaseAdvisor_PlanPromptUsesAbsoluteArtifactPath`).
- **`sanitizeAdvisorTier` accepts only `fast`, `balanced`, `deep`, or `top`.** Legacy model names are rejected (`TestSanitizeAdvisorTier`, `TestSanitizeAdvisorTier_RejectsHighAndRawModel`).
- **A regular file at the workspace path is treated as nothing to archive.** `archivePollutedWorkspace` leaves non-directories as no-ops (`TestArchivePollutedWorkspace_NotADirReturnsNoOp`).
- **An unrecoverable ship failure preserves the worktree; a recovered ship cleans it up as normal.** Integrity aborts keep audited diffs while recovered ships prune normally (`TestOrchestrator_ShipFailureAborts_PreservesWorktree`, `TestOrchestrator_ShipRecoversThenSucceeds_CleansWorktree`).
- **`model_routing=auto` is the only mode projecting `{cli,tier}` onto `PhaseRequest`.** `advisory` and `static` persist `phase-plan.json` without modifying dispatch (`TestModelRouting_AutoApplies`, `TestModelRouting_AdvisoryLogsNotApplies`, `TestModelRouting_StaticIsNoop`).
- **An advisor outage degrades model routing to the static per-phase profile instead of aborting.** Dispatch proceeds with empty CLI/tier overlays (`TestModelRouting_AutoDegradesToProfileStatic`).
- **The two `model_routing_*` wire fields on `PhaseRequest` are `omitempty`** so zero-value requests keep existing JSON bytes.
- **Missing mandatory-phase handoffs record `spine-unsatisfied-warn` at Enforce without blocking.** Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_Enforce_SpineUnsatisfied_WarnsButProceeds`.
- **Phase-timing entries capture `started_at`/`ended_at` by bracketing dispatch.** Pinned by `TestPhaseTiming_EndStrictlyAfterStart_AdvancingClock`.
- **The `--resume` path (`RunCycleFromPhase`) stamps `started_at` too.** Pinned by `TestPhaseTiming_ResumeStampsStart`.
- **The upfront-plan `RouteInput` passes `IntentRequired` to `ClampPlanToFloorWith`**, preserving `EVOLVE_REQUIRE_INTENT=1` (`TestOrchestrator_ThreadsIntentRequiredToPlannerInput`).
- **The tree-diff guard snapshots every dispatched phase, not only worktree-gated ones.** Non-worktree leaks still trigger the guard outside `isLegitimateMainTreePath` (`TestGuardCatchesInsertedPhaseLeak`, `TestGuardIgnoresLegitimateWorkspaceWrite`, `TestIsLegitimateMainTreePath`).
- **The guard and `recoverBuildLeak` share one legitimacy vocabulary for worktree-phase writes.** Pinned by `TestGuardIgnoresOrchestratorSelfWrite_WorktreePhase`.
- **A worktree phase cannot smuggle a deliverable out by rename.** Even when the new path looks legitimate, the old path keeps the guard armed (`TestGuardCatchesDeliverableRenameSmuggle_WorktreePhase`).
- **Inserted/minted-phase leak coverage exercises the end-to-end seam** (`parsePhasePlan` → `ClampPlanToFloorWith` → `registerMintedPhases` → dispatch → guard; `TestInsertedPhaseMainTreeLeakRecovers`, `TestInsertedPhaseWorktreeWriteIsClean`).
- **An unregistered phase skips with a WARN only when genuinely Optional.** Mandatory missing runners still fail as wiring bugs (`TestRunCycle_UnregisteredOptionalPhase_SkippedLoudly`, `TestRunCycle_UnregisteredMandatoryPhase_StillFatal`).
- **No phase dispatch path runs cwd=main-tree, and abnormal aborts preserve worktrees.** `RunCycle`, `RunCycleFromPhase`, and out-of-band retro all enforce this (`TestCB1_ResumePathCarriesWorktree`, `TestCB1_FailureLearningRetroCarriesWorktree`, `TestAbortCleanupPreservesWorktreeDiff`).
- **The orchestrator splices `phase-complete` checkpoints into `.evolve/cycle-state.json` after each phase.** Failed phases stay out of `completedPhases` (`TestOrchestrator_PhaseBoundaryCheckpoint`, `TestOrchestrator_FailedPhase_NoSuccessCheckpoint`).
- **A review-gate rejection sets `abort_reason` without rewriting agent verdicts.** Exhausted retries synthesize FAIL (`TestPhaseOutcome_AbortPaths_AlwaysRecordTimingAndUsage`, `TestPhaseOutcome_NeverInventsPass`).
- **A cycle's outcome label comes only from its own ship latch, never external HEAD movement.** Own ship wins over retro advisories (`TestFinalizeOutcome_SkippedWithoutOwnShip_NeverShippedViaBuild`, `TestFinalizeOutcome_OwnShipTrumpsAdvisory`).
- **The ship latch (`CycleState.Shipped`) is written when ship PASSes and survives pause/resume.** `completeCycle` forwards it to `finalizeCycle` (`TestRunCycle_ShipPassPersistsTheShipLatch`, `TestResumeLifecycle_ShipPassPersistsTheShipLatch`, `TestCompleteCycle_ForwardsTheShipLatchToTheOutcomeLabel`, `TestResumeLifecycle_PostShipSkippedVerdictReadsTheCheckpointLatch`, `TestRunCycle_EmptyTriageCommitmentSurvivesSiblingLanding`).
- **A failed phase's structured failure block flows verbatim into deterministic retro and lesson YAML.** Synthesized summaries run only when self-reporting is absent (`TestRecordFailureLearning_StructuredBlockFlowsIntoArtifacts`).
- **An OPTIONAL phase exhausting retries on an infra error degrades to WARN and advances.** MANDATORY phases and non-infra failures stay cycle-fatal (`TestOptionalPhaseInfraTimeoutSkipsAndCycleShips`, `TestMandatoryPhaseInfraTimeoutStaysCycleFatal`, `TestOptionalPhaseNonInfraErrorStaysCycleFatal`).
- **The static `nextInOrder` fallback never runs a phase vetoed by `Run: false`.** Even after `enforceNext` declines a jump, vetoes hold (`TestOrchestrator_AdvisoryPlanVetoSurvivesSpineDecline`).
- **The router runs under an observer like every phase.** `observedPlan` and `observedRePlan` (`router_observer.go`) start the observer under `routerAgentLabel` and watch `router-pane-watch.json` (`TestRouterObserver_InitialPlanRunsUnderARouterObserver`, `TestRouterObserver_PostScoutRePlanRunsUnderARouterObserver`).
- **`WithObserver(nil)` keeps `noopObserver` instead of assigning nil.** Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_WithNilObserver_FallsBackToNoop`.
- **Build explanation sealing waits for host-side normalization.** Audit sees gofmt-normalized Build output (`TestOrchestrator_NormalizesBuildBeforeSealingExplanationForFirstAudit`).
- **`CycleRequest.Env` propagates to every `PhaseRequest.Env`** so phases read `EVOLVE_CLI` and `EVOLVE_*_MODEL` (`TestOrchestrator_CycleEnv_PropagatesToEveryPhase`).
- **`CycleState.IntentRequired` persists** for resume and classifier checks (`TestOrchestrator_IntentGate_DefaultRunsScoutFirst`, `TestOrchestrator_IntentGate_PhaseEnableRunsIntentFirst`).
- **Intent-required resolution precedence is Context over Env**: `Context["intent_required"]` overrides the environment knob.
- **`PlanJudge` is not a routing brain and fails open by signature.**
  - It uses `judge` on the fast tier (`claude-tmux`/`haiku`), returning `Score=-1` on missing bridges, errors, or bad output without aborting.
  - Pinned by `TestRouteQualityJudge_RecursionGuarded`, `TestRouteQualityJudge_ScoresPlanAgainstGoal` and `TestRouteQualityJudge_FailOpenToMinusOne`.
- **`phaseRole`'s audit/build mappings (`audit`→`auditor`, `build`→`builder`) are load-bearing strings.** Other phases map to themselves (`TestPhaseRole`).
- **`PhaseRequest` and `PhaseResponse` round-trip through `encoding/json` exactly.** Pinned by `TestPhaseRequest_JSONRoundTrip`.
- **`VerdictPASS`, `VerdictFAIL`, `VerdictWARN`, and `VerdictSKIPPED` are the only allowed verdict strings.** Pinned by `TestVerdict_Recognised`.
- **The phase-timing dossier and durable log project one composed record.** `flushPhaseTimings` appends resumed segments exactly once without deduplicating repeated dispatches (`TestFlushPhaseTimings_*`).
- **The phase-timing recorder is a lazy Null Object until a Signal Center is wired.** Bare literals get one lazily, while `NewOrchestrator` wires eagerly and sees late `WithSignalCenter` calls (`TestRecorder_*`).
- **`phase-timing.json` has one writer: `cycleRun.flushPhaseTimings`.** `TestPhaseTimings_SingleWriter` scans non-test Go files outside `internal/core/outcome/` for extra callers of `WritePhaseTimings`.
- **`comparePhaseIOShadow` re-derives expected values directly from `router.RoutingSignals`.** It checks every `HandoffsFromSignals` field (`TestComparePhaseIOShadow_CoversAllProjectedFields`).
- **Each phase's shadow-equivalence test pins its exact legacy `Context` keys** (`phaseio_shadow_test.go`), testing `assembleCycleInputs` and `assembleErrorContext` directly.
- **`compareCycleInputsShadow` applies `phaseio.CapField` bounds to legacy values** so over-cap strings never raise false mismatches (`TestCompareCycleInputsShadow_*`).
- **The per-cycle phase-output survey lives in `internal/core`.** `RunCycle` and `RunCycleFromPhase` both emit `EventPhaseOutputsSurveyed` through `internal/phaseoutputs`, taking the last event per cycle (`TestRunCycle_EmitsPhaseOutputsSurveyToUnifiedStream`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_EmitsPhaseOutputsSurvey`).
- **`IsShippingVerdict` is the one definition of shipping verdicts.**
  - `shippedOutcome` requires a moved HEAD and notifies `ThroughputRecorder` (`WithThroughputRecorder`), avoiding duplicate cycles through `hasThroughputCycle`.
  - Pinned by `TestShippedOutcome`, `TestThroughputRecorderWired_Probe`, `TestIsShippingVerdict_WholeOutcomeVocabulary` and `TestIsShippingVerdict_IsTheOneDefinitionShippedOutcomeUses`.
- **`State` and `CycleState` sub-records alias `cyclestate` leaf DTOs** (`ports.go`), keeping JSON shapes owned by the leaf without breaking callers.
- **`BridgeRequest.Skills` and `SystemPrompt` share one producer→adapter split.** Config resolves policy while the bridge adapter injects content into prompts.
- **A legacy string-`cycle` `LedgerEntry` normalizes to `cycle:0` plus `cycle_label` on re-marshal.** Pinned by `TestLedgerEntry_RoundTrip_StringCycle_NormalizesToLabel`.
- **`repinShipSHAAfterBuild` re-pins `expected_ship_sha` after successful builds** through `phaseintegrity.RepinIfDrifted` when provenance verifies, skipping unverified changes and absent binaries (`TestBootRecovery_*`).
- **`MergeWorkspacePrescriptionCarryover` merges OPEN `"PRESCRIPTION: "` rows into `state.CarryoverTodos` at `finalizeCycle`** regardless of continuation binding (`TestRunCycle_MergesPrescriptionCarryoverIntoState`).
- **The all-families-quota-exhausted short-circuit runs before `recordFailedApproachState`.**
  - It leaves phase state, `FailedAt`, and P0 carryover untouched while matching `ErrAllFamiliesExhausted` with `errors.Is`; ship walls still feed `ShipFailReasons`.
  - Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AllFamiliesExhausted_*`, `TestRunCycle_NonQuotaDispatchFailure_StillDispatchesRetroOnce`, `TestRecordFailureLearning_*` and `TestDispatch_SingleFamily85WithSibling_FailureLearningUnchanged`.
- **`recurrence.Ledger.RecordClosure` is wired at deterministic retro closeout** (boundary-only escalation with nil `Escalator`/`Autofiler`, pinned by `TestC662_RetroCloseoutRecordsClosureInLedger`).
- **A remediated gate's re-run deliverable passes through review before `recordAndBranch`.** Pinned by `TestRemediation_GateReRunIsReviewed`.
- **`composeRepairBrief` leads with `CycleState.AuditFailReasons` (`runnerDiagnosedAudit`), then verdict defects, then findings.**
  - Findings sort CRITICAL/HIGH then MEDIUM (`reportdoc.Findings`), flagging repeats from `audit-report.round<N-1>.md` (`reportdoc.FindingKey`).
  - `archiveRepairPrompts` archives prior prompts as `<phase>-prompt.round<N>.txt` without overwriting existing archives.
- **`repairRoundTier` raises tdd/build repair dispatches to `model_tier_overrides[audit_retry_2plus]`** when `AuditRepairActive` holds, clamped to the envelope on live and resume paths (`TestRepairRoundTier_*`, `TestRepairRoundDispatch_*`, `TestRepairRoundResume_*`).
- **A colliding remediation slug never suppresses the retrospective, lesson, or defect inbox items.** Disambiguation hashes full defect text (`TestRepro1285_F1_CollidingRemediationSlugSuppressesRetrospectiveAndLesson`).
- **`SealCycle` (`reset.go`) preserves stuck cycle history.** It moves the workspace to `<workspace>.reset-<UTCnano>/`, writes `reset-manifest.json`, bumps `lastCycleNumber`, appends a `cycle_label` ledger row, removes `cycle-state.json`, and updates `state.json` as an untyped map.
- **`AutosealStaleMarker` auto-seals dead-PID markers at loop boot using `SealCycle(Force)`.** `markerShouldAutoseal` probes PID liveness and sets `SealOptions.AutomatedRecovery` (`TestMarkerShouldAutoseal_*`, `TestAutosealStaleMarker_*`).
- **`SealOptions.AutomatedRecovery` sets role `operator-autoseal`** so dead-owner boot recovery cannot mint operator-signed ledger anchors.
- **`SealCycle` takes `<state.json>.lock` across `failurelog.Record` and its state RMW.** That serializes concurrent `storage.UpdateState` callers without lost updates (`TestSealCycle_ConcurrentUpdateStateNoLostUpdate`, `TestSealCycle_ManyConcurrentUpdateStateWritersNoLostUpdate`, `TestSealCycle_LockReleasedPromptlyAfterCompletion`).
- **`reset_concurrency_test.go` lives in `core_test` to avoid an import cycle** because `storage` imports `core`.
- **A resolved fleet-rebase conflict never re-audits or reships on the stale base.** Unresolved conflicts stop the cycle (`TestRecoverFromShipError_ADebuggerResolvedConflictReentersTheRebaseNotAStaleReship`, `TestRecoverFromShipError_AConflictTheDebuggerLeftEndsTheCycleWithoutAReship`).
- **A cycle ended by the debugger ends FAIL** (`BLOCK`, empty decision, or re-entry abort), releasing its inbox claim and recording a ship fail reason (`TestRecoverFromShipError_*`).
- **`SealCycle` resolves a worktree's `state.json` symlink for both the lock and RMW without severing the link.** Pinned by `TestSealCycle_SymlinkedStateLocksCanonicalTarget`.
- **`SealCycle` fences sealing on `runlease`, never `.evolve/.lock`.** Fresh heartbeats refuse with `ErrCycleOwnedLive` unless `--force` (`TestSealCycle_RegressionCycle395`, `TestSealCycle_LeaseFencing`).
- **`LoadResumeState` falls back to a fleet per-run scan only on `ErrNoCheckpoint` without an active lane override.** Stale primary checkpoints do not fall through (`TestLoadResumeState_*`).
- **Only an escalation-pause checkpoint is resumable by another process; `phase-complete` never is.** Pinned by `TestLoadResumeState_PhaseCompleteBreadcrumbIsNotDiscoverable`.
- **`discoverPerRunResumeState` excludes candidates with fresh `runlease` records and resumes the newest valid sibling.** Pinned by `TestLoadResumeState_FreshLeaseExcludesCandidate`, `TestLoadResumeState_StaleLeaseDoesNotExclude` and `TestLoadResumeState_NewestStaleFallsBackToOlderValidAndSaysSo`.
- **Discovery refuses a checkpoint that a later launch shipped past, and names the stopped lanes (2026-10-21, inbox item `resume-after-a-fleet-signal-stop`).**
  - In wave 27 a SIGINT stopped lanes 1739 and 1740, which wrote no checkpoint. `--resume` then picked the quota checkpoint of cycle 1709 from the day before. Its `gitHead` was `unknown`, so the HEAD check did not apply.
  - A run counts as finished when its run state is `end` or `aborted`, or when the ledger holds a landed ship for it. `landedShips` reads that row: `role: ship`, `kind: phase`, `exit_code: 0`. A shipped run state stays at phase `ship`, so the phase string alone does not say it shipped.
  - A checkpoint is stale when a later cycle of another launch (another `goal_hash`) shipped after its `savedAt` (`shippedAfter`). It stays resumable after a pool refill of the same launch, a ship before the pause, or a later launch that failed. The refusal names the discard command `EVOLVE_CYCLE_STATE_FILE=<path> evolve cycle reset`.
  - A checkpoint is also stale when a later launch of another goal started after its `savedAt` (`startedAfter`, 2026-10-21). The stopped lanes of that launch are the evidence. A run without `started_at` cannot be judged this way.
  - A checkpoint older than policy `resume.max_checkpoint_age_h` (compiled default 192 h, one weekly quota reset plus a day) is stale too.
  - When `origin/main` has only moved past the checkpoint's base, the checkpoint stays resumable, and the resume logs a WARN with the commit count (`warnMainMoved`). Main moves several times an hour through console merges. Ship's stale-base rebase (`staleBaseAsRebase`) lands the resumed cycle. The claim judge (`CheckpointResumable`) keeps the paused lane's claim.
  - The checkpoint writers (`internal/checkpoint`) record the base as `gitHead`. The base falls back to the run's `worktree_base_sha` or `pre_cycle_head`. When `origin/main` cannot be read, the log says why. The check reads the local `origin/main` ref and does not fetch.
  - Each stale refusal names `EVOLVE_CYCLE_STATE_FILE=<path> evolve cycle reset`. It names the deliberate override `EVOLVE_CYCLE_STATE_FILE=<path> evolve loop --resume` only for use while `evolve inbox claims` still shows the item held. An explicit state file logs a WARN that it waives the age check.
  - Pinned by `TestLoadResumeState_RefusesACheckpointThatALaterLaunchStartedAfter`, `TestLoadResumeState_AMainThatOnlyMovedResumesWithAWarning`, `TestLoadResumeState_RefusesACheckpointOlderThanTheConfiguredAge`, `TestCheckpointResumable_AMovedMainKeepsTheCheckpointAndAllowHeadMovedWaivesTheAge`, `TestLoadResumeState_TheStateFileOverrideWaivesTheAgeBoundVisibly` and `TestBoundaryCheckpointers_RecordTheCycleBaseAsGitHead`.
  - `perRunVerdict` judges each unfinished run: a live lease, no enabled checkpoint, a reason that is not resumable, or stale. `refusalList` names the stopped lanes and the stale checkpoints, up to 20, then a count. It sums the checkpoints that are not resumable by reason, such as `phase-complete 173`.
  - `evolve cycle reset` renames the run dir to `<dir>.reset-<stamp>` (`resetArchiveMarker`), and discovery skips such an archive. A `savedAt` that is not RFC3339 leaves the checkpoint resumable, and the log line says that the stale rule did not apply.
  - Pinned by the tests in `resume_signal_stop_test.go`. They write the shapes of real runs: a shipped run at phase `ship` with its ledger row, and a stopped lane at phase `build`.
- **A resumed cycle writes its next checkpoint back to its resume origin, not the host singleton.**
  - `ResumePoint.StatePath` records that file (singleton or lane per-run file).
  - `ActivateResumeStatePath` points resolution there through `ipcenv.CycleStateFileKey` and returns a restore func (no-op if unchanged).
  - Without it, a pause writes to the singleton while discovery checks the per-run file (`TestActivateResumeStatePath_RoutesResolutionToTheDiscoveredFile`, `TestActivateResumeStatePath_NoOpWhenAlreadyResolved`).
- **`RunCycleFromPhase`'s worktree teardown runs before storage-lock release and reads live closeout state at defer.**
  - `clearActiveWorktree` updates persisted state under the held storage lock, so teardown defers before lock-release (LIFO runs teardown first).
  - The closure reads `execution.closeout` at defer time so early returns see zero values and preserve the worktree.
  - Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_PrunesItsWorktreeOnNormalCompletion`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_PreservesAFailedCycleWorktree` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_PreservesAnAbnormallyExitedWorktree`.
- **A resumed deliverable uses the fresh loop's skip set, and version-0 checkpoints add no extra skip.**
  - `mandatoryExplanationReviewer` already delegates on version 0. Skipping version 0 at resume exempted contract and deliverable gates too ([ADR-0100](../adr/0100-declared-deliverables-gate.md) §4).
  - Pinned by `TestBuildFloorReviewer_LegacyCheckpointVersionZeroIsExempt`.
- **`RunCycleFromPhase` appends `role=auditor kind=agent_subprocess` and `role=builder kind=agent_subprocess` ledger entries.** Both bind to the resumed git HEAD so ship never reads stale bindings (`TestRunCycleFromPhase_EmitsAuditBinding`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_EmitsBuildBinding`).
- **`RunCycleFromPhase` accepts any phase in `o.runners`, not only the 13 `Phase.IsValid()` spine phases.** Advisor-inserted phases (for example `mutation-gate`) register through `MintPhase` and can resume; `PhaseEnd` and `PhaseStart` stay rejected (`TestRunCycleFromPhase_InsertedPhaseInRunnersAccepted`).
- **A resumed advisor-inserted phase restores the transition kernel from `routing-plan.json` instead of the static state machine.**
  - `RunCycleFromPhase` reads `{"phase","run"}` entries to find the next phase after the inserted one.
  - When missing, the cursor degrades the inserted phase to its archetype instead of returning `core: invalid phase`.
  - Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_InsertedPhaseTransitionsViaPlan` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_MissingPlanDegradesNotInvalidPhase`.
- **A resume-loop transition failure must run through `recordPhaseOutcome` before returning.** The stalled phase gets `<phase>-usage.json` with `abort_reason`, recording `FAILED_EXPLAINED` instead of [ADR-0044](../adr/0044-unified-phase-recovery-protocol.md) C1 `FAILED_UNEXPLAINED` (`TestRunCycleFromPhase_TransitionFailureRecordsAbortReason`).
- **The resume loop (`resumeExecution.run`) mirrors fresh per-phase behavior as a first-class dispatch surface.**
  - It stamps `PhaseStartedAt`, passes `ActiveWorktree` and `RunID`, resets `PhaseAudit` reasons, retires prior verdict artifacts, and awaits build normalization.
  - It latches ship checkpoints like `applyPostReviewGuards` and calls `decideAfterAuditFail` so repair retries stay reachable during recovery.
  - Its history branch calls deterministic `decideAfterRetro` directly, while the fresh loop adds advisory `decideAfterRetroRouted`.
  - `seedAuditRepairContext`, `consumeAuditRepairGrant`, and `consumeBookkeepingRegradeGrant` key on `next`, because keying on `current` missed post-repair TDD runs.
  - Resume omits the debugger signal override (ADR-0058 S3) and exits through `debugger→end`.
  - Pinned by `TestResumePostDispatchExits_RecordTheirOutcome` and `resume_parity_*_test.go`.
- **Resume post-dispatch errors record through `recordPhaseOutcome` without triggering failure-learning.** Feeding retro from one arbitrary error exit spends extra retro runs, so resume error exits are recording-only (`TestResumePostDispatchExits_RecordTheirOutcome`).
- **`timingOwner` uses `context.Background()` for flushes, but iteration escapes copy the live `ctx` before `recordChokepointEscape`.**
  - `recordChokepointEscape` calls `recordFailureLearning`, which skips retro only when its context is cancelled.
  - All four mutated fields (`cs`, `state`, `result`, `phaseTimings`) read back so `abnormalEpilogue` never overwrites retro completions (`TestResumePostDispatchExits_RecordTheirOutcome`).
- **Resume's terminal `WriteCycleState` after `completeCycle` is resume-only.** If it fails, `cycleCompletedNormally` still prunes the spent tree. A later `--resume` then fails loudly on the missing worktree instead of re-running phases.
- **`normalizeWorktreeToBase` checks ancestry before resetting, because rebase recovery can orphan the persisted base.**
  - `reset --soft` to a non-ancestor creates spurious diffs, so non-ancestors warn and skip. Both loops share this normalize step.
  - Pinned by `TestNormalizeWorktreeToBase_SkipsNonAncestorBase`, `TestNormalizeWorktreeToBase_Idempotent`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_NormalizesBuildWorktree` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_NoNormalizeWhenResumingPastBuild`.
- **Resume's history branch calls `finalizeRetroCompletion` where the fresh branch does.** Absent or invalid `disposition.json` always surfaces `disposition-gate:` in `RetroDecision`, never verified clean (`TestRunCycleFromPhase_HistoryBranchSurfacesDispositionGate`).
- **The table in [2026-09-11-large-component-decomposition-plan.md](../../reports/2026-09-11-large-component-decomposition-plan.md) lists intentional differences between fresh and resumed loops.** Other differences are parity gaps to close.
- **`applyDispatchPolicy` raises retried scoped items to `deep` after routing projection, even under `model_routing=static`.** Envelope limits still clamp the raise (ADR-0076 D). Pinned by `TestEscalatedBuildTier_RaisesAtThresholdEnvelopeless`, `TestEscalatedBuildTier_RaiseOnlyNeverLowers` and `TestEscalatedBuildTier_EnvelopeMaxClampsThroughRealGuardrail`.
- **The next build's tier is the higher of two named rules: the ladder target and the ADR-0076 floor (shadow-stage contract F6).**
  - `nextItemDecision` builds a complete `retryladder.Input` through `ladderInput`, the one builder that `askLadder` also uses. It then calls `retryladder.Next`.
  - The class is the worst class of the scoped items (`worstScopedClass`): `capability` first, else the first class.
  - No code outside `retryladder` builds a `Decision` or `Verdict` literal. When no scoped item has a class, nothing is asked and no event is emitted.
  - The decision is recorded once per cycle from origin `cycleRun.escalatedBuildTier`. `cs.Ladder` takes the rung and the target of that decision.
  - The old copy `nextItemStartRung` and the mapping of `cs.Ladder.Tier` to `deep` are deleted.
  - The count threshold (`buildFloor`) is no longer an entry gate. It is the floor predicate and keeps its stderr signal.
  - `composeBuildTier` returns the higher tier of the floor tier and the ladder target. The target counts only at `enforce` and only for a `raise`.
  - An `unknown` failure at R0 now starts raised at R1. `retryladder.Next` places a `capability` failure at R0. It goes to R1 if an effort step is open and the fingerprint is new. Else it takes the tier climb.
  - `nextItemDecision` is a pure query of one class from the R0 start rung, and `ladderTarget` joins the class, the query and the record. `recordStartRung` writes the cycle state and emits the event once. The floor line goes to the error writer, and only when the floor applies.
  - Pinned by `TestEscalatedBuildTier_TheLadderTargetFeedsTheDispatchedTierOnlyAtEnforce`, `TestEscalatedBuildTier_TheFloorSignalNamesTheFloorOnly`, `TestEscalatedBuildTier_UnknownClassStartsNotStop`, `TestNoDecisionLiteralOutsideDecider`, `TestComposeBuildTier_TheHigherOfTheLadderTargetAndTheFloor`, `TestWorstScopedClass_CapabilityWinsThenTheFirstClass` and `TestEscalatedBuildTier_TheLadderDecidesTheStartRungAndTheFloorStillRaises`.
- **A Build dispatch's `BudgetScale` carries the difficulty multiplier of the cycle** (ADR-0076 slice A).
  - The engine uses it to stretch the artifact-wait deadline. A scale of 1.0 stays unset, so legacy dispatches stay byte-identical.
  - The `cycle_size_estimate` signal is the existing scout and triage vocabulary. `router.Digest` parses it from `triage-report.md`, so no agent contract is new.
  - The same signal scales the contract-correction limit of the BUILD phase only (`correctionLimitFor`). Audit and adversarial-review keep their configured ladder, because judgment budgets do not depend on difficulty.
  - `scaledCorrectionLimit` rounds half-up and clamps to `policy.MaxContractCorrectionRetries`. It never shrinks the base. A multiplier of 0 or less is the identity, so a missing signal never zeroes the ladder.
  - Pinned by `TestBuildBudgetScale_FromTriageReport`, `TestBuildBudgetScale_AbsentReportIsUnscaled`, `TestSizeBudgetMultiplier_MapsAndDefaults`, `TestScaledCorrectionLimit_RoundsAndClamps`, `TestScaledCorrectionLimit_BuildPhaseOnly` and the composed proof `TestSizeBudget_ComposedLargeCycleExtendsCorrectionLadder`.
- **The archetype-to-phase mapping of `degradeInsertedSuccessor` never skips the audit gate.**
  - A plan-role archetype (a planning phase that the advisor inserts) resolves to Build.
  - A build or evaluate archetype (for example `bug-reproduction` or `fault-localization`) resolves to Audit. This mirrors the real bugfix flow.
  - A control archetype resolves to Ship. An unknown archetype defaults to Audit, the safe integrity funnel.
- **`gitWorktree.Cleanup` refuses to run when its worktree path resolves to the project root.**
  - `git worktree remove --force` fails harmlessly there. The `os.RemoveAll` that follows then deletes the repository.
  - The refusal is `sameDirectory`. It matches by inode when both paths exist, and by text when one does not. Thus a path that git cannot stat but that has the same text still refuses.
  - The check lives in the provisioner, beside the `cycle-` gate of `deleteCycleBranch`. Thus every caller of `Cleanup` is covered, not only the exit path of the orchestrator.
  - This matters on the resume path. `ActiveWorktree` comes from a saved checkpoint, not always from `worktree.Create`, and can name the project root. `shipDirect` of `ship` branches on the same shape.
  - Pinned by `TestGitWorktreeCleanup_RefusesTheProjectRoot`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ProjectRootCheckpointSurvivesTeardown` and `TestSameDirectory`.
- **`writeDeterministicLearning` files an inbox item only for a defect that the failure itself reports. It never files a copy of the synthesized summary.**
  - A classed `FailureBlock` with zero `Defects` leaves the synthesized summary as the only entry. It files nothing.
  - A `Defects` list with one entry that only repeats the summary is the same shape. It also files nothing. Both restate the failure and are not actionable.
  - The retrospective is written in both cases. Only the inbox filing stops.
  - The call site reuses `faillearn.structuredDefects`, the one named rule for this shape. It does not derive the check again.
  - Pinned by `TestWriteDeterministicLearning_ClassedButDefectlessBlockFilesNothing`, `TestWriteDeterministicLearning_EchoDefectListFilesNothing` and `TestWriteDeterministicLearning_StructuredDefectsAreFiled`.
- **Each correction directive asks for a numbered record. A correction of a source writer also asks the bridge for the worktree-evidence contract** ([ADR-0113](../adr/0113-correction-completes-on-worktree-evidence.md)).
  - `composeCorrection(round, reason, remediation)` ends both branches with `correctionRecord(round)`. The agent appends a `## Correction N` section that names what it fixed and where, also when the fix is in another file. Thus the next review and every later reader see what each correction changed. A deliverable that is not Markdown is written again in full.
  - The request states only what the agent must write and why. How the bridge decides completion stays out of the prompt.
  - Both ladders thread their own ordinal: `corr` in `reviewWithCorrections` and `correction` in `reviewResumedDeliverable`. The salvage retry passes the same round through `composeContractSalvageRetry`.
  - `PhaseRequest.BridgeCompletion` is the one rule for who may complete on worktree evidence. That is a request with a correction directive, a worktree and no `Fence`. The fence comes from the orchestrator's projection, so audit, adversarial-review and every unregistered phase are excluded. Every other request keeps the artifact contract, and core still judges the carried deliverable through the ordinary review.
  - Pinned by `TestComposeCorrection_AsksForAnAppendedSectionNumberedByTheRound`, `TestCorrectionRecord_StatesTheAgentsDutyNeverTheHostsCompletionRule`, `TestCorrectionLadder_EachReDispatchAsksForItsOwnNumberedSection`, `TestCorrectionCallSites_PassTheRemediation` (it also scans `resume.go`) and the three `TestPhaseRequest_BridgeCompletion_*` tests. The rule's file, `bridge_completion.go`, is on the protected-surface manifest of the guards.
- **The remediation fix directive asks for the same numbered record.** `maybeRemediate` in `cyclerun_remediate.go` ends the builder fix directive with `correctionRecord(round)`, numbered by the remediation round. The builder appends a `## Correction N` section to its report. The placeholder check of the build floor already parses that heading (`correctionHeadingRE`). The read-only gate re-verify directive is unchanged. Pinned by `TestRemediation_FixDirectiveAsksForTheRoundsNumberedCorrectionRecord` and `TestRemediation_RequestedRecordRoundTripsThroughThePlaceholderCheck`.
- **The completion contracts are one typed vocabulary** (`ports.go`).
  - `CompletionContract` declares `CompletionArtifact`, `CompletionStdout`, `CompletionGit` and `CompletionWorktreeEvidence` once. It sits beside `BridgeRequest.Completion`, which carries that type.
  - The zero value `""` is the artifact contract. Only the tmux drivers of the bridge honor a contract. The headless drivers ignore it.
  - The phase judge, the failure advisor and the retry adjudicator name `CompletionArtifact`. The advisor package cannot import core, so it keeps its own string and `bridgeRequestOf` converts it at the seam.
  - The values are wire names. The bridge reads them from `--completion` and `BRIDGE_COMPLETION`, and they travel in the JSON of the request.
  - Pinned by `TestBridgeRequest_EachCompletionContractTravelsAsTheNameTheBridgeParses`, and from the bridge side by `TestCompletionContractVocabulary_SpelledOnce` and `TestCompletionContractVocabulary_EveryRequestNamesItsContractByTheTypedConstant`.
- **The two branches of `composeCorrection` differ in how they forbid collateral changes.**
  - The branch for a gate-authored remediation relays it verbatim. Only the rejecting gate knows what "fixed" means for its own violation.
  - That branch omits "do not change unrelated files". Some remediations must create an artifact that the deliverable is not, and that clause forbids the requested fix.
  - The default branch keeps the no-collateral instruction. There the artifact exists and needs only a correction.
  - The rejection `reason` string must survive byte for byte and appear once. It carries the `[code] message` tokens of `deliverable.summarize()`.
  - `contractViolationCodeRE` (`contract_escalation.go`) parses those tokens back out of the directive downstream. The re-dispatched agent reads the literal violation text.
  - A lossy reformat, a cut or a copy breaks both consumers at once.
  - Pinned by `TestComposeCorrection_CarriesReasonVerbatim`, `TestComposeCorrection_FramingSurroundsTheReason` and `TestComposeCorrection_EmptyReasonStillProducesADirective`.
- **`backoffSleep` is a package var, not an `Orchestrator` field.**
  - `TestMain` can then zero it once for the whole test suite. Each retry test does not need to inject a no-op sleep at construction.
  - `executeRetryBackoff` is a free function. A per-instance field forces per-test churn that the package var removes.
  - It is set once before any test runs, so concurrent reads by parallel tests are safe.
- **`computeRetryEnvelope` is the single retry authority of the category table of ADR-0072. Nothing else decides legality.**
  - It replaces two things that disagreed. `fp.Categories[...]` was read for `Level` alone, and Action, MaxRetries and FixType were unused. ADR-0092 built a parallel `max_audit_repair_attempts` knob and a disposition-prose eligibility rule that reached the same cap of 2 on their own.
  - It is a pure predicate: it does no I/O, and the policy is passed in. Thus the live, routed and resume paths get the same answer from one table.
  - A declared class normalizes through `failurelog.NormalizeLegacy` before the lookup. The repo runs two vocabularies: `infra-systemic` and `transport-hang` in policy, `infrastructure-systemic` and `exit-transport-hang` in failurelog. The legacy `audit-fail` and `FAIL` both mean `code-audit-fail`. Without normalization, a retryable class is declined by mistake.
  - A system-level declared class does not halt at this chokepoint. Both floor gates require `IsFloor`.
  - An agent writes the declared class, so it is unauthenticated and uncorroborated. It must not stop the batch on prose alone. Non-floor system categories such as transport-hang and non-progress can stop it that way.
  - A decline routes to retro, where the full two-gate floor decides. Only the deterministic floor candidate (gate 1) halts here.
  - Pinned by `TestComputeRetryEnvelope`, `TestComputeRetryEnvelope_CapComesFromThePolicyTable`, `TestComputeRetryEnvelope_ZeroCapDisablesRetry` and `TestComputeRetryEnvelope_DeclineReasonsNameWhichVocabularyFailed`.
- **`policyCategoryFor` is the one join between two vocabularies.**
  - The failurelog vocabulary has 13 classes. The audit contract renders it and the deliverables gate enforces it.
  - The policy vocabulary has 7 categories. The retry table uses them as keys.
  - Before this join, the envelope looked up a failurelog spelling in the policy table and declined it as "unrecognised class". The audit prompt itself recommended some of those words.
  - A known classification joins to a category that the default table knows, or to `""`.
  - `""` means a failure that no rebuild repairs. Examples: a human abort, an operator reset, an integrity breach, a ship-gate config, a transient outage, a rejected intent or a fatal loop.
  - The envelope declines it by name, not as unrecognised.
  - Pinned by `TestPolicyCategoryFor_JoinsEveryKnownClassificationOrSaysNone`.
- **The liveness fence of `SealCycle` checks the PID, not only the freshness of the lease.**
  - `SealOptions.PidAlive` passes a liveness probe to the fence through `runlease.OwnerLive`.
  - An owner whose process is dead but whose lease heartbeat is fresh now seals without `--force`. A live owner (fresh lease, live pid) still refuses.
  - A nil `PidAlive` keeps the old freshness-only fence for a caller that did not migrate.
  - Pinned by `TestSealCycle_DeadOwnerFreshLease_SealsWithoutForce`, `TestSealCycle_LiveOwnerFreshLease_StillRefuses` and `TestSealCycle_NilPidAlive_PreservesFreshnessOnlyBehavior`.
- **`shiperror.go` is a back-compat shim over `internal/shiperr`, a leaf with no dependency.**
  - Type aliases, const re-declarations and thin func wrappers keep the call sites of `core.ShipError`, `core.Code*`, `core.NewShipError` and `core.AsShipError` unchanged.
  - Ship (the constructor) and core (the matcher) both import `shiperr` in one direction.
  - Every ship error code must stay reachable through `core`, not only `shiperr`. The consumers, the ship phase and the debugger-routing layer, import `core`.
  - Pinned by `TestCodeManifestGate_DualRegistered` and `TestCodeRepoContractGate_DualRegistered`.

- **`seedTaskRecall` always replaces the inherited recall context, also for a no-match result, on both dispatch paths.**
  - A scoped lane cannot reuse the memory of an unrelated task on resume.
  - It reads the existing durable lesson corpus only. It persists no second mutable memory summary.
  - Pinned by `TestTaskRecall_ReplacesStaleMemoryWithoutMutatingCaller`, `TestAdvisorPlanInput_RecallsGoalWithoutHistory` and `TestDispatch_TaskRecallWithoutFailureHistory` (integration tier).

- **`ValidateSafetyInvariants` is the load-time trust anchor for the ship floor** (ADR-0060). The legality graph and the gates are config. Thus the check of the floor covers graph and config *roles* (mandatory anchors, verdict branches), never phase names. Any phase can then be renamed without a weaker floor. Each check and its pinning test:
  - Branch targets (`on_pass`/`on_fail`) must name a known phase that is a legal successor; config selects among legal edges and never invents one (ADR-0058): `TestValidateSafetyInvariants_IllegalBranchTarget`, `…_UnknownBranchTarget`.
  - A config spine (`SpineOrder`) must join known phases by legal edges. `Next` walks the spine and does not check `CanTransition` again. An illegal jump such as scout→ship then routes around an anchor: `…_IllegalSpineEdge`, `…_UnknownSpinePhase`.
  - `legal_successors` must name only known phases on both sides. `legalGraphFrom` drops an unresolvable name with no sign, so a typo degrades the graph with no load error: `…_UnknownLegalSuccessor`, `…_UnknownLegalSuccessorTarget`.
  - A mandatory phase whose artifact gate constrains the verdict may accept only shippable verdicts (PASS/WARN), so a config cannot ship a FAILed evaluation: `…_FloorGateAcceptsOnlyShippableVerdict`.
  - Some mandatory phase must gate on a shippable verdict (F⊆M). Without one, `SpineSatisfiedUpTo` admits ship with no verdict gate. A presence-only gate with an empty `verdict_in` does not count. The check runs only when the catalog describes a mandatory phase. A synthetic catalog in an orchestration unit test cannot describe the gate of the evaluator: `…_NoMandatoryEvaluatorRejected`, `…_PresenceOnlyEvaluatorRejected`, `TestSpineSatisfiedUpTo_ConfigurableMandatoryWeakensGate`.
  - Every mandatory anchor must be reachable from the start node, and a graph with no start node is itself a violation: `…_StrandedMandatoryAnchor`, `…_NoStartNodeRejected`.
  - Every start→ship path must traverse a floor evaluator. Ship (the last mandatory anchor, by registry convention) must be unreachable once the whole evaluator set is deleted from the graph. Only the evaluator carries the verdict floor. Thus `SpineSatisfiedUpTo` backs the non-evaluator anchors at run time: `…_EvaluatorBypassRejected`, `…_TwoEvaluatorsCollectiveDominance`. The reference flow passes (`…_ReferenceFlowPasses`).
- **Per-cycle worktree provisioning isolates the source-writing phases (tdd, build) from the live tree.** A failed or buggy cycle cannot change it. The only write allowance of the role gate is `phase==build && ActiveWorktree!=""`. See ADR-0027.
- **`gitWorktree.Create` names the branch and the directory on the stable per-root lane, not on the run ID of each invocation.** Thus concurrent sibling worktrees of one repo never collide on a global `cycle-<N>` branch, and the worktree name survives resume. Pinned by `TestGitWorktree_ConcurrentSiblingsNoBranchCollision` and `TestGitWorktree_GuardStateIsRunScoped`.
- **A reused worktree is revalidated (`git rev-parse --git-dir`) and quarantined clean before its cycle resumes.** A pruned ref can leave a stale directory that `os.Stat` sees and git rejects. The uncommitted dirt of a prior failed try can otherwise ride into the audited tree of the reused cycle. Pinned by `TestEnsureCleanWorktree_QuarantinesInheritedOrphanRedTest` and `TestEnsureCleanWorktree_RepeatQuarantineNeverOverwritesSalvage`.
- **`gitWorktree.Create` always cuts a named branch, never `--detach`.** Worktree-aware ship resolves the cycle branch with `git symbolic-ref --short HEAD` and fast-forward merges it to main. A detached HEAD gives an empty branch, and ship fails. Pinned by `TestGitWorktree_CreateUsesNamedBranch`.
- **A fresh lane is based on the current upstream tip, resolved only on the create path.** The reuse path never rebases a live lane, because that discards work in progress. Pinned by `TestGitWorktree_Create_ReuseSkipsFetch` and `TestGitWorktree_Create_FetchesOriginBeforeBasingLane`.
- **`git worktree add` retries on lock contention.**
  - Every fleet lane shares one `.git`. Concurrent `git worktree add` calls can collide on the repo-level lock and return rc=255 with no stderr beyond "Preparing worktree".
  - The provisioner treats that as retryable. After the last try it still fails loudly with the same error.
  - The retry announcement says "retryable", not "transient". It fires for each failure that the classifier does not rule out, and that is not the same as an established contention case.
  - Pinned by `TestWorktreeAddRetry_LockCollisionStillRetriesToBound`, `TestWorktreeAddRetry_AnnouncementDoesNotClaimUnclassifiedTransience`, `TestGitWorktreeCreate_RetriesTransientAddFailure` and `TestGitWorktreeCreate_PersistentFailureStillFailsLoudly`.
- **`laneStartRef` never bases a fresh lane on the HEAD of the local checkout.**
  - In a multi-lane fleet, the shared checkout falls behind the remote as sibling lanes land. So it fetches origin and cuts from the resolved integration head.
  - A fetch failure fails loudly. It does not base the lane on the stale local tip. A repo with no remote falls back to `"HEAD"`.
  - Pinned by `TestLaneStartRef_IntegrationHeadAuthority`, `TestGitWorktree_Create_NoOriginFallsBackToLocalHEAD` and `TestGitWorktree_Create_FetchFailureIsFatal`.
- **A local `main` ahead of origin is a lane base only when every ahead commit is proven (2026-10-21).** `unprovenAhead` (`landed_main.go`) reads `origin/<branch>..<head>` and accepts two commit kinds.
  - A boundary commit changes only `knowledge-base/cycles/`, `.evolve/inbox/` or `recoveryretro.DocsHome` (`docs/retrospectives/`): a dossier closeout, the loop's inbox stamps or a recovery retrospective.
  - The boundary publishes recovery retrospectives since 2026-11-10, and the list did not name their home. On 2026-11-11 two of them deferred all 16 lanes of wave 166. The list now takes the home from `recoveryretro`, and `TestLaneStartRef_ARetrospectiveThatTheBoundaryPublishedIsAProvenBase` runs the real publisher.
  - A merge is accepted when origin holds each of its other parents, as for the `evolve sync-main` merge.
  - Any other commit, such as a strand from an older binary or a manual commit, makes `laneStartRef` refuse the base and name the commit. The lane then defers.
  - The check trusts a merge by its parents and does not read the merge's own conflict resolution. The first two prefixes come from a survey of 200 closeout and stamp commits on origin/main (2026-10-21). The third is `recoveryretro.DocsHome`, which the boundary step publishes since 2026-11-10.
  - `rebaseCycleBranchOntoMain` runs the same check before the fleet rebase. An unproven commit refuses the rebase, so the identity carry and ship never treat it as a landed peer (cycle 1830). A fault while reading the range is a refusal too.
  - Pinned by `TestLaneStartRef_IntegrationHeadAuthority`, `TestRebaseCycleBranchOntoMain_AnUnpushedCodeCommitOnLocalMainIsNotALandedPeer`, `TestRebaseCycleBranchOntoMain_BoundaryCommitsAndASyncMergeStillCarry` and `TestUnprovenAhead_AFaultIsNotAProof`.
- **`laneStartRef` fetches only the base branch, and retries ref-lock contention.**
  - It resolves the default branch first (`originDefaultBranch`) and fetches it alone. It uses `gitexec.FetchOriginBranch` with the explicit refspec `+refs/heads/<branch>:refs/remotes/origin/<branch>`.
  - One bare store backs the runtime plane, every fleet lane and every console dev worktree. Any of them can fetch at the same moment.
  - A bare `git fetch origin` updates every remote-tracking ref. Contention on any unrelated ref used to fail the base of the lane. In cycle 1806 it was a train branch that `gh pr update-branch` had just moved.
  - Contention on the base ref itself (`cannot lock ref`, `unable to update local ref`) is retried up to `gitexec.DefaultFetchAttempts` with the shared 2 s and 4 s backoff.
  - The backoff clock is the `worktreeAddRetrySleep` seam that the add retry also uses. Each retry is announced as `[worktree] retry N/M: git fetch origin <branch> after ref-lock contention rc=…`.
  - The branch is the default of the remote, never a hard-coded `main`.
  - Pinned by `TestLaneStartRef_FetchesOnlyTheDefaultBranch`, `TestLaneStartRef_FollowsTheRemotesDefaultBranch` (origin/HEAD → `origin/trunk`), `TestLaneStartRef_RetriesRefLockContention`, `TestLaneStartRef_PersistentContentionFailsAfterTheBound` and, against real git with a held `.lock` file, `TestLaneStartRef_RealLockOnAnUnrelatedRefDoesNotFailTheBase` and `TestLaneStartRef_RealLockOnTheBaseRefIsRetried`.
- **A fleet lane gets two provisioning tries before it defers.**
  - In fleet mode, `WorktreeProvisioner.Create` can fail after its own fetch and add retries. Then `newCycleRun` calls `reprovisionWorktree`. It logs a `re-provisioning once` WARN and calls `Create` again.
  - `Create` is idempotent. The second call reuses a valid half-built worktree or replaces an invalid one.
  - A sequential cycle calls `Create` once, as before. It runs on best effort with no worktree, so a second try gains nothing.
  - Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AReprovisionedFleetLaneRunsNormally` and, for the single sequential call, `TestRunCycle_ASequentialCycleWithoutAWorktreeKeepsItsBestEffortRun`.
- **A provisioned worktree is checked before the cycle uses it.**
  - `verifyFreshCycleSource` refuses a worktree that already holds an occupied copy of the identity of this fresh cycle. The check runs after the upstream fetch.
  - The refusal ends the cycle before its state is persisted or any phase dispatches. The refused worktree is preserved. `Create` may have reused it and does not say if it did.
  - The comment that said this in `newCycleRun` was removed on 2026-10-06 with its stale "creation remains best-effort" sentence. It is archived in [the comment history](../../history/code-comments/internal-core.md).
- **A fleet lane that still has no worktree is DEFERRED before any phase dispatches. It is never run and never FAILed.**
  - In fleet mode (`EVOLVE_FLEET=1`) the bridge refuses every launch without an explicit worktree (`fleet mode: explicit worktree required`, exit 10).
  - Before, the dispatch ran anyway and sealed the cycle FAIL. It wrote a failure digest, ran failure learning, queued a lesson about the task and counted toward the consecutive-failure rules. The cause was an infrastructure fault (cycle 1806).
  - Now `newCycleRun` returns a `*LaneDeferral{Cycle, Cause}` before it persists the cycle state.
  - `deferLaneWithoutWorktree` records one host ending: phase `start`, verdict `SKIPPED`, abort reason `lane-worktree-deferred: <cause>`. It writes it through `cycleRun.flushPhaseTimings`, the one timing writer.
  - Thus `cyclehealth.ClassifyOutcome` reads the cycle as `DEFERRED`, the existing deferral outcome that the quota wall also uses.
  - The seal is `cycle.sealed` WARN `ORCHESTRATOR_LANE_DEFERRED` (`fields.step=worktree`, `fields.cause`).
  - The cycle writes no failure digest, `audit-fail-reason.json`, failure-learning carryover or retrospective. Thus neither the digest rules of the pipeline-blocker breaker nor the consecutive-FAIL count of the operator sees a task failure.
  - `evolve cycle run` releases any claim of the cycle with no failure bump and exits `fleet.ExitDeferred` (5). The wave counts that as deferred, not failed.
  - A sequential cycle keeps its best-effort run. There the bridge has the project-root fallback, and the failure digest records the provisioning cause as before.
  - Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AFleetLaneWithoutAWorktreeDefersBeforeAnyDispatch` and `TestRunCycle_ASequentialCycleWithoutAWorktreeKeepsItsBestEffortRun`.
- **A deferral that does not clear halts the batch.**
  - A deferral writes no failure digest. Thus the digest rules of the pipeline-blocker breaker never see it.
  - A persistent provisioning fault defers every lane of every wave until `--max-cycles`. Examples are a stale `refs/remotes/origin/main.lock` in the shared store, a network or credential outage, or any deterministic `Create` error.
  - `CollectBatchLaneDeferrals` reads the run dirs of the batch through `cyclehealth.ClassifyOutcome`. It keeps only the `DEFERRED` cycles whose abort reason has the `lane-worktree-deferred` prefix. Thus a quota DEFERRED never counts.
  - `EvaluateLaneDeferrals` halts on `failure_policy.thresholds.lane_deferral_halt_ceiling` consecutive deferred cycle numbers. The default is 3, a non-positive value keeps the default, and policy cannot disable the rule. The rule is `lane-deferrals`, and the reason names the last git cause.
  - A cycle between them that did not defer (a ship, a FAIL) breaks the run.
  - A deferred cycle is transparent to the `consecutive-failures` run. `LaneDeferredCycleSet` passes the deferred cycles of the batch in `BlockerBreakerConfig.LaneDeferredCycles`, and `consecutiveRun` steps over them (`adjacentOver`). They neither count nor break it.
  - Thus a lane that defers between failing lanes cannot hide the failures: F D F D F halts as `consecutive-failures`. A ship still breaks the run.
  - Both rules use one counter, `consecutiveRun`. The run-dir walk is shared with `CollectBatchFailureDigests` (`batchCycleNumber`).
  - A deferral whose record cannot be written (the workspace cannot be created) is not returned as a deferral. `deferLaneWithoutWorktree` returns a plain error that names the unrecorded deferral and the provisioning cause. Thus the cycle fails visibly, and does not defer where the breaker cannot see it (`TestRunCycle_ADeferralThatCannotBeRecordedFailsLoudly`).
  - Pinned by `TestCollectBatchLaneDeferrals_CountsOnlyWorktreeDeferralsInTheBatch`, `TestEvaluateLaneDeferrals_HaltsOnlyOnAnUnbrokenRun` and `TestConsecutiveFailures_LaneDeferredCyclesAreTransparent`; the halts themselves by `cmd/evolve`'s `TestBlockerBreakerHalt_ConsecutiveLaneDeferralsHaltWithTheGitCause` and `TestBlockerBreakerHalt_AlternatingDeferralsAndFailuresHalt`.
- **Console hand-offs past a ceiling in a rolling window halt the loop** (`no_work_handoff_breaker.go`, inbox `no-work-hand-off-rate-alarm`). Since F30 a triage that empties `top_n` by drops ends its lane as planned no-work (exit 0). No FAIL rule counts the item that it hands to the console. `CollectNoWorkHandoffs` reads the last `window` cycle dirs above the acknowledged mark. It keeps those whose `committedset.HandedOff` record names a routed item, so an empty-backlog no-work cycle does not count.
  - `EvaluateNoWorkHandoffs` halts at the ceiling with the fingerprint `NoWorkHandoffFingerprint(last)`. `NoWorkHandoffsResolvedThrough` reads the highest acked cycle from the resolved-fingerprints ledger.
  - Pinned by `TestCollectNoWorkHandoffs_ReadsTheLastWindowOfCyclesAboveTheAckMark`, `TestEvaluateNoWorkHandoffs_HaltsAtTheCeilingWithAnAckableFingerprint` and `TestNoWorkHandoffsResolvedThrough_IsTheHighestAckedCycle`.
- **`integrationHead` resolves the base of a fresh lane to the branch that the landing targets, not always `origin/<default>`.**
  - The remote tip is authoritative off the default branch, or when the local main is current or behind. The wave boundary fast-forwards the local main.
  - The local main is authoritative when it sits strictly ahead, because its unpublished landings ride the next push.
  - A diverged history is refused loudly. It does not pick either side.
  - Pinned by `TestLaneStartRef_IntegrationHeadAuthority`.
- **`linkGuardDeps` symlinks the live dispatcher binary and the guard-read state files into every fresh worktree.**
  - The PreToolUse hooks of the trust kernel run `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/go/bin/evolve guard ...`. The harness pins `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` to the cwd (the worktree), never to a value that is set before.
  - A fresh checkout has neither the binary nor the `.evolve/` state. They must be linked in, or the hooks fail and the source phases stall.
  - `cycle-state.json` links to the `run.json` mirror of the run, not to the host-global file. Concurrent runs hold whichever file the last run wrote. The link can dangle for a short time, until the first `WriteCycleState` resolves it.
  - A failed link is logged, not swallowed. A missing state link can let a guard read an empty state and wrongly allow a denied tool.
  - Pinned by `TestGitWorktree_GuardStateIsRunScoped`.
  - The `state.json` link is also the cycle-worktree mark that `inboxmover` reads. A mutating inbox verb refuses when its inbox directory sits beside that link, and names the plane root. Thus no `inboxmover` verb moves the tracked inbox copy of the worktree ([internal-inboxmover.md](internal-inboxmover.md)). Pinned by `TestProvisionedWorktree_ItsTrackedInboxCopyRefusesTheMover`.
- **The cross-lane mint exemption verifies content, not only registry membership.**
  - The registrar records a minted name in the shared `mintregistry` before it persists the files. Thus a mint of a concurrent fleet lane that lands mid-phase is exempt from the tree-diff guard by name.
  - A registered name alone buys nothing. The guard also parses the on-disk `phase.json` again against the same spec-side clamp that the registrar enforces. The file must parse, be valid at the user floor, be optional, and have a name that matches the directory.
  - Thus a forged registry entry with an unclamped spec still aborts loudly.
  - A legitimate mint writes exactly `phase.json`. Any companion file under a registered name is still a leak.
  - A corrupt registry file is quarantined at once (renamed aside). It does not degrade the exemption fleet-wide, and the next read sees a clean empty registry.
  - A mint older than `mintregistry.TTL` no longer exempts its path. Thus the registry cannot decay into a standing allowlist.
  - Pinned by `TestGuardExemptsConcurrentLaneMintedPhaseConfig`, `TestGuardStillAbortsUnregisteredPhaseConfigLeak`, `TestGuardAbortsForgedRegistryEntryWithUnclampedSpec`, `TestGuardAbortsCompanionPayloadUnderRegisteredMint`, `TestGuardQuarantinesCorruptRegistryAndStaysArmed` and `TestGuardStillAbortsExpiredMintPhaseConfigLeak`.
- **`truncateGoal` and `maxGoalTextChars` project the goal-text cap of the advisor. They are consumers, not a second definition.**
  - The goal section of the judge and the digest of the task recall both render through this projection. It is `textcap.TruncateRunes` applied at `advisor.MaxGoalTextRunes` (4000).
  - Thus core never re-implements the bound of the advisor.
  - Pinned by `TestTruncateGoal_ProjectsTheAdvisorCap`.
- **The advisor plan never offers a phase whose persona doc is known to be missing.**
  - Before it makes the plan, the orchestrator probes every runner that is catalog-Optional, non-floor and implements `PersonaProber`. It hands the absent ones to the router as `RouteInput.UnavailablePhases`.
  - Mandatory and floor phases are never probed. Their absence stays a loud dispatch failure and is never a silent exclusion.
  - `unavailablePhaseReason` treats a wrapped `ErrAgentDocMissing` as the one deterministic reason to exclude a phase.
  - These cases leave the phase selectable. A runner has no `PersonaProber`. A phase has no runner. A probe fails with no known absence. Thus dispatch shows the real cause, not a guess at plan time.
  - Pinned by `TestAdvisorPlanInput_ListsPhasesWithMissingPersona` and `TestWriteRoutingContext_UnavailablePhasesAreNamedNotOffered`.
- **`VerdictReason` does not replace the wire types.**
  - `PhaseResponse.Verdict` and `FailedRecord.Verdict` keep their bare string for the stability of the ledger, the hash chain and the JSON.
  - `VerdictReason` comes from the `Diagnostics` of a phase. It carries the extra signal that the orchestrator needs to react (classify → PROCEED/RETRY/BLOCK) and that the advisor needs for recall.
  - `ReasonFromDiagnostics` projects the Summary through `cyclestate.ErrorMessages`, the one rule that every reason surface uses. Thus the verdict identity of the ledger never names a FAIL differently from the `FailedRecord`, the line of the chokepoint log or the seal.
  - `Taxonomy.Consequence` has the type `failureadapter.Classification`, not a free string. A mismatch with the vocabulary of the failure adapter is then a compile error, not a surprise at run time.
  - `Source` and `FailureMode` stay free strings. They are descriptive and are not control flow.
- **The source-write axis is declared twice on purpose.** The two homes are `writes_source` in the phase registry, and the `WorktreePhase` literal as the catalog-less floor of the role gate.
  - The role gate (`internal/guards`) has only the `CycleState` and no catalog. It relies on the fixed literal for the built-in source writers. This is defense in depth, and it is identical to the config value.
  - `TestRegistry_DeclaresWriteAxisForSourceWriters` iterates the content of the loaded registry, with no hard-coded phase name. Thus a drift between the two homes fails the test.
- **The `os.RemoveAll` of `gitWorktree.Cleanup` never runs against a path that this provisioner did not create.**
  - `deleteCycleBranch` runs a non-force `git branch -d` only. It relies on the merged-check of git as its safety net, and does not escalate to `-D`.
  - It is gated on the `cycle-` leaf prefix. Thus Cleanup never deletes the branch of an unrelated path.
  - Pinned by `TestCleanup_DeletesMergedCycleBranch` and `TestCleanup_UnmergedBranchSurvives_WarnsOnly`.
- **`resolveWorktreeBaseSHA` records the HEAD of a reused worktree verbatim as `WorktreeBaseSHA`.**
  - If HEAD is a salvage snapshot, it walks back to the first non-snapshot ancestor.
  - The snapshot SHA as a base makes `normalizeWorktreeToBase` soft-reset onto the commit that salvage protects. The preserved diff then reads as empty.
  - The walk applies only to a snapshot HEAD. A walk-back for every HEAD re-bases each ordinary lane.
  - When HEAD is readable but its ancestry is not, the captured HEAD is still returned verbatim with a WARN, not an error. An empty base disables normalization, and that is the worse outcome.
  - The missing-base scenario of `TestVerdictCacheCollisionRegression` depends on this fail-open path. It stubs `rev-parse HEAD` and needs the capture to survive a failed ancestry walk.
  - A chain that is snapshots all the way down has no honest base. It fails loudly and does not fall back to the snapshot or to an empty string.
  - Pinned by `TestWorktreeReuseBase_SalvageSnapshotHEADResolvesToFirstNonSalvageAncestor`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_SalvagedWorkIsPendingInTheReviewDiffAfterNormalize`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_StackedSalvageSnapshotsResolveToTheCommitBeneathAll`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_OrdinaryHEADIsRecordedVerbatim`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_CommitMentioningSalvageInBodyIsNotTreatedAsASnapshot`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_UnresolvableSnapshotAncestorFailsLoudly`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_UnreadableAncestryRecordsHEADVerbatim`, `TestNewCycleRun_UnresolvableSnapshotBaseFailsProvisioning` and `TestWorktreeReuseBase_ProvisioningPathRecordsGuardedBaseInCycleState`.
- **`inPlaceWorktree` is the one predicate that every worktree mutator checks before it touches a worktree.**
  - The mutators are `normalizeBuildWorktree`, `recoverBuildLeak`, `worktreeContentSHA`, `snapshotPreservedWorktree`, `rebaseCycleBranchOntoMain`, the `Cleanup` of the provisioner and the teardown message.
  - A cycle never normalizes, reverts, stages, commits, rebases or removes the project root of the operator. This includes a symlink alias or a relative spelling of it.
  - An empty `projectRoot` is unknown, never the root.
  - Pinned by `TestWorktreeMutators_RefuseTheProjectRoot`.
- **Failure routing and breaker rules.**
  - A guard abort is pipeline machinery that failed. It is never a task defect, so its pre-class is `guard-class`.
  - A digest whose fingerprint asserts no defect identity is "unexplained". Distinct failures collapse into such buckets by construction, so the identical-fingerprint rule never counts them. The unexplained rule owns them.
  - The consecutive-failures rule is evaluated last. When two rules trip together, the more specific rule names the halt.
  - An acknowledged fingerprint is excluded from the identical-fingerprint count and from the consecutive streak. A resumed batch that counts it will halt again before the fix is verified.
  - Cycle numbers are batch-global. A fully failed wave of width 2 adds 2 to the streak. A missing digest is a PASS and splits a streak.
  - `ParseConsumptionFingerprint` needs the literal `fingerprint` token before the triplet, so a pipe-delimited path never matches. It fails closed.
  - The ack ledger `.evolve/resolved-fingerprints.json` is written by a tmp-and-rename, so no reader sees a partial ledger. A missing ledger is an empty set.
- **Audit round bookkeeping.**
  - The previous round's verdict artifacts are retired before the pre-phase cycle-state write on both dispatch surfaces. A crashed round's verdict is therefore retired on resume.
  - If archiving fails, the stale file is removed. A surviving verdict is replayed.
  - A counter rollback can leave an archive in place. The first retirement's evidence is never overwritten.
  - `AuditRepairAttempts` is a monotonic counter, not a flag for "repairing now". Audit re-reads its own artifacts, so re-injecting its own rejection is circular.
  - A bookkeeping regrade matches only at the start of a record, so a quoted occurrence inside another message cannot match. Producers never emit a `narrative=FAIL` conflict line. The matcher rejects one that is forged.
  - A FAIL with green artifacts and no `AuditFailReasons` is the signature of a forged verdict.
- **Audited tree.** The tree is the one on the auditor row that ship binds. A run without such a row gives an empty entry, not an error.
- **Boot quarantine.** Boot recovery re-pins the ship binary in the same pass. Quarantining it reopens the SHA mismatch that the re-pin heals. Git can report a collapsed untracked parent directory that `classifyDirtyPaths` cannot exclude, so the pathspec excludes the ship binary too.
- **Console lease.**
  - The lease lives in the git common dir, outside every worktree, so no lane phase can write or shadow it.
  - An absent, malformed, expiry-less or expired lease reads as an empty set, and an unresolved hub gives no path. The guard stays armed in every degraded shape.
  - `expires_at` is required. No expiry means no lease.
  - The lease is adopted once at cycle start, so a write in mid-cycle cannot waive its own cycle. The logged digest comes from the bytes that were adopted, not from a second read.
- **Build floor.**
  - The floor diffs against the cycle base. The builder commits its work, so a HEAD diff is empty at review time and the floor approves with nothing checked.
  - The protected-surface check turns rename detection off. A file moved out of the surface is judged by its old path too. A nil predicate fails open, and the ship tripwire stays armed.
  - Changed packages are tested after `reviewAndGuard` normalizes gofmt and derived projections, so the tested tree is the tree that Audit receives.
  - An added package that is gated by build tags is invisible to the default run. It runs under its own tags. An enforced package runs once, in the coverage pass of the apicover naming check.
  - That coverage pass needs a coverage profile. Without one, every named export reads as covered.
  - Only violations in files that the change touched fail hard. Older debt in a touched package only warns.
  - A failure to run is green, and downstream gates stay armed. Every fail-open branch warns.
  - A package output keeps its tail, because `go test` writes its FAIL lines, panics and stack traces last. Complete output carries no elision marker, so the marker stays trustworthy.
  - The documentation floor classifier drops test-only diffs, and the verdict stays WARN.
- **Build report checks.**
  - The removal check parses only the claim block. Prose is never parsed, because a matcher on the word "removed" blocks honest reports.
  - A claim with an absolute or escaping path is not adjudicated. A claim that is absent and untracked, or a Git failure, fails open.
  - A placeholder token is `UPPER_SNAKE_PLACEHOLDER`. No persona emits one, so this grammar is the only home of the rule.
  - The persona budget check fails only when a persona document was touched and the prompts package is red. It skips when `changedPackageFloorChecks` already runs the package.
- **Carry-forward screen.**
  - Supersession is checked first. A dry-run merge of a change that is already absorbed reads clean.
  - The check uses `git merge-tree --write-tree`, which is a real three-way merge that writes only to the object database. Exit code 1 is a conflict. The bare one-argument form reports clean on conflicts.
  - A candidate that is not landable is either superseded or a conflict. Calling a conflict "landed" drops overlapping work in silence, so supersession is screened again.
  - A conflict routes to the debugger and never short-circuits.
- **Composition ladder.**
  - A composed-tree gate decline falls back to a full re-audit at all three trip sites (rung 0, rung 2 and the identity carry-forward).
  - The rung-2 reviewer is trusted only when its resolution keeps the audited patch-id. A language-model merge is verified, never trusted. An unknown verdict value fails closed.
  - `compositionPatchID` and `CompositionVerdictInput` mirror the ledger adapter, which core cannot import.
  - `WithCompositionGateRunner` takes a function that returns statuses and output tails. The orchestrator field keeps its status-only map, and the option projects statuses and hands the full outcomes to the per-call sink.
  - The snapshot and the gate runner and the verdict writer must all be bound for the fast path. A partial binding is not wired.
- **Continuation (salvage and resume).**
  - Salvage snapshots use a fixed committer identity, so preservation never depends on the git identity of the host.
  - A snapshot stages everything that the run produced, with `add -A`. The staging discipline of a declared manifest applies to ship binding, not to salvage.
  - A clean worktree is idempotent and returns HEAD. A failed merge of the base is aborted and returned. Stale code never reaches Build. A cancelled context must not leave an unresolved merge in preserved work.
  - Only a snapshot that the screen classifies as Clean is stamped. AlreadyLanded has nothing to resume. Conflict belongs to the debugger path.
  - Findings come from `audit-fail-reason.json`, which carries the actual reasons. `failure-digest.json` is only an identity shell. Reasons render as lines, not as escaped JSON.
  - Findings are cut at the last line break inside the budget. A byte cut can land inside a rune or leave half a finding. An unreadable findings file warns loudly, so "no findings existed" differs from "the manifest points at the wrong path". The continuation still adopts, because the work matters more than the notes.
  - A declined continuation releases its registry binding. If the binding stays, it outlives its lineage, and the out-of-band check of the defect-ledger gate fails every later lane on that scope. Only the orchestrator releases. A binding for another ancestor cycle is untouched. A failed release leaves the binding, so the next lane declines again.
  - A lane whose scope came from the wave planner has no inbox item. Its manifest is also registered under each pinned todo id. A blank id is skipped, because `materializeLaneScope` splits an empty scope into one empty string. Every failure is a WARN, because salvage bookkeeping never fails cycle finalization.
  - Adoption runs right after triage, when this cycle's claims exist on disk. A failure before seeding keeps fresh provisioning. A failure after seeding stops the cycle and preserves the worktree.
- **Contract escalation.**
  - Escalation triggers on the gate's own consecutive-block count (`contractEscalateAtBlock`), not on a local ordinal. The breaker is one global file, so an aborted cycle leaves it hot.
  - Block identity is the set of violation codes in the reason. Two blocks are one defect when their code sets intersect. When a block yields no codes, identity falls back to the fingerprint-normalized reason of the blocker breaker.
  - The rule is "prior reason known and different suppresses". It is never "equal escalates", because a hot breaker can start a ladder at block 2 with nothing to compare. The zero value reports true.
  - The code pattern is narrow (no spaces), so bracketed prose in a message cannot fake a code.
  - `contractArtifactDetermined` is an allowlist and fails closed. A repair that deletes a stray copy leaves the watched artifact byte-identical, so an unchanged hash is not proof of inaction. It has no production caller yet. `TestContractArtifactDetermined_CodesMatchDeliverableVocabulary` keeps its codes equal to the deliverable vocabulary, because `internal/deliverable` imports core.
  - The salvage retry runs only when no other CLI family exists. It adds no dispatch and consumes no extra correction, so the breaker count is untouched. A remediation from the gate replaces the clause that forbids edits to other files, because a remediation exists for violations whose fix creates something.
  - A demotion is staged as an intent, never written to `.evolve/inbox`. A write in mid-flight races `inboxmover.Claim` and can revive a claimed item. `recurrence.ApplyBoundary` is the only sanctioned inbox writer. The ledger entry keeps `salvage_attempted` beside `escalated`. A ledger or staging failure never changes the outcome. The intent weight comes from policy, with a compiled default when the load fails.
  - A demotion is reported even when a later gate in the chain rejects the same deliverable.
  - The universal fallback CLI equals the llmroute default. `TestUniversalContractFallbackMatchesLLMRouteDefault` pins it.
- **Correction ladder (salvage rung).**
  - Salvage relocates only into the workspace or `<ProjectRoot>/.evolve`. The basename check bounds the file name and this bounds the directory, so a faulty verifier cannot make salvage write elsewhere.
  - `withinRoot` refuses an empty root. Without that rule, a missing `ProjectRoot` can admit every path in the tree as a destination.
  - The search order is worktree, workspace, then cwd. A phase that writes source most likely misplaced its report in the tree that it edits. A file is relocated first, by an atomic rename, and the destination is verified after.
  - A candidate must be a regular file (lstat, no symlink), non-empty, no larger than 1 MiB and fresh. An unknown phase or a reject that is not shaped like an artifact does nothing.
  - The evidence digest has kernel-verified facts only: names from `git status` and the original path of a stray. `gitStatusNames` keeps stdout untrimmed, because the first porcelain line starts with a status column.

### Cycle driver rationale (dispatch, correction, closeout, covering tests)

- The covering-tests corpus (`covering-tests.md`) removes the whole-repo Grep of test-amplification. The corpus must be on disk or announced. `ensureCoveringTests` closes the resume-past-build hole. `writeCoveringTests` runs only on a fresh `PhaseBuild` completion.
- Derivation of the corpus is fail-open. A corpus already on disk is a silent no-op. A truncated corpus warns on stderr, so a trimmed corpus is never taken for a complete one. `renderCoveringTests` is the single source of the omitted count.
- `sanitizeCorpusPath` replaces backticks and newlines. It does not escape them. The path set comes from the worktree diff, so it is attacker-influenced. The phase agent treats the file as AUTHORITATIVE. Replacement keeps the byte-cap arithmetic exact (one rune in, one rune out).
- `recordCrossArtifactInvariants` is advisory, best-effort and loud. A write failure warns and is ignored. It runs before the verdict-coherence floor. A finding can then be read beside the floor decision and never changes it.
- `finalizeOutcome` credits `SHIPPED_VIA_BUILD` only from the ship latch of the cycle (`CycleState.Shipped`). `latchShippedState` sets it after the deliverable review approves. Movement of main HEAD is never evidence, because a sibling fleet lane moves HEAD all the time. The latch lives in the checkpoint, so a pause and resume after ship cannot lose it.
- `teardownCycleWorktree` is the one disposal rule for fresh and resumed cycles. It prunes only spent trees. A preserved tree holds audited work that can be uncommitted. A cycle that did not complete normally also keeps its tree.
- `evolve loop --resume` and `evolve cycle reset` reclaim the tree by its path. A missed prune costs disk. A wrong prune destroys audited work. `gitWorktree.Cleanup` refuses the project root.
- `ClearCompletedCycleMarker` removes only a COMPLETED marker (`cycle_id <= lastCycleNumber`) that has no live lease owner. It never changes `state.json`, never archives and never writes a lesson. Those duties belong to `SealCycle` on abandon. `readLastCycleNumber` reads the raw field. A missing `state.json` reads 0, so an in-progress marker never counts as completed.
- Dispatch treats a missing built-in or mandatory runner as fatal, because it is a wiring bug. It skips a non-dispatchable optional user phase loudly. Every phase runs with cwd set to the cycle worktree. The cwd is not write permission. The role gate, the tree-diff guard and `worktreePhase` own the write axis. A re-dispatch clears the diagnosed-FAIL explanation of the prior try.
- The failure arms of dispatch run in a fixed order. All-families quota exhaustion (exit 85) comes first. It checkpoints a resumable pause and spends no more tries. Artifact backfill (exit 81 only) comes next.
- Then the optional-phase infra skip, which gives a WARN and never applies to a floor or mandatory phase. Then ship-error recovery, with bounded depth. An integrity breach falls through to the abort. The post-ship observer skip comes last. It accepts any error shape, but only after ship has landed.
- Every degrade stays in failure-learning and the ledger. An unparseable verdict is fatal to the cycle only for a floor or ship phase. Dispatch records the outcome before the failure-learning retro, so the timing record stays in order.
- `countDispatch` (`dispatch_attempt.go`) sets `PhaseRequest.Attempt` before each launch of a phase in the cycle. The launches are the first dispatch, an infra relaunch, a contract correction, a remediation round and a retried evaluate member. So two correction rounds of build write `attempt` 1, 2 and 3 in `llm-calls.ndjson` (ADR-0142 L0, [retry-ladder §6](../retry-ladder.md#6-the-try-record)). The members of a parallel evaluate batch are different phases that dispatch at the same time, so a mutex guards the count. A resumed run keeps one count for all its iterations and its review gate corrections, until a new process restarts it at 1. Pinned by `TestDispatchAttempt_AResumedCorrectionContinuesTheCount`, `TestDispatchAttempt_AResumedRunCountsItsCorrectionsAfterTheResumedDispatch`, `TestDispatchAttempt_EveryPhaseOfAResumedRunSharesOneCount`, `TestDispatchAttempt_ARemediationRoundIsTheNextDispatchOfTheGateAndTheBuilder`, `TestDispatchAttempt_AnEvaluateMembersInfraRelaunchIsItsNextAttempt`, `TestDispatchAttempt_TwoCorrectionRoundsAreAttemptsOneTwoAndThree`, `TestDispatchAttempt_AnInfraRelaunchIsTheNextAttempt` and `TestDispatchAttempt_ParallelEvaluateMembersCountWithoutARace`.
- A build that the review gate rejects after its corrections is recorded as a FAIL with the `BUILD_DELIVERABLE_REJECTED` diagnostic (`rejectedOutcome`, 2026-10-21). The message is the abort reason. Before, the entry kept the PASS of the builder and carried no code. Then the retro's PASS was the last entry, and the cycle classified as system-level, so the item was released without a charge. A rejection of another phase keeps its uncoded outcome. Pinned by `TestReviewWithCorrections_ABuildRejectedAfterItsCorrectionsRecordsACodedFail` and `TestReviewWithCorrections_ARejectedPhaseOtherThanTheBuildKeepsItsUncodedOutcome`.
